    🏗️ Arquitetura

    • Arquitetura
    • Decisões (ADR)
      • Git, repositório e política de branches
        • ADR 0001 — Formato do contrato normalizado do GitProvider
        • ADR 0002 — Normalização de erros do GitProvider
        • ADR 0003 — Política de retry para providers remotos
        • ADR 0004 — Cadastro de credenciais de git do usuário
        • ADR 0005 — Bootstrap de Gitflow idempotente e retomável
        • ADR 0028 — Proteção de branch: divergência entre providers e a matriz de aprovação
        • ADR 0030 — Política de branches mecanizada
        • ADR 0044 — Adoção de repositório existente, e o plano como portão
        • ADR 0056 — O engine trabalha em repositório remoto
        • ADR 0139 — O alarme de merge de esteira ganha destinatário, e a regra de `dev`
        • ADR 0165 — O repositório nasce no aceite do handoff ao Arquiteto, e a execução não começa sem ele
        • ADR 0217 — Promoção automática `dev` → `qa` enquanto a QA está em fase de teste
        • ADR 0235 — A `qa` é aposentada por hora e a esteira do repositório fica `dev → main`
        • ADR 0236 — O Dependabot dispensa revisão na `dev` e fica mergeável com os outros checks verdes
        • ADR 0255 — A política de PR é obrigatória em todos os repositórios e a PR de regeneração do bot dispensa a escada na dev
      • Harness de agente, turnos e sessões
        • ADR 0006 — Harness de agentes: montagem determinística de contexto
        • ADR 0007 — ToolLoop, ferramentas, ContextManager (harness com LLM)
        • ADR 0061 — O tipo da sessão é dado da criação, e a execução continua sendo evento
        • ADR 0098 — Limites de transporte e janela efetiva de compactação coerentes
        • ADR 0163 — O clique responde ao ACEITAR o comando, e o turno segue pelo canal
        • ADR 0175 — O Infra Lead conversa pelo composer, e antes o turno dele passa pelo `TurnoAssincrono`
        • ADR 0177 — O idioma das respostas mora em três lugares — conta, sessão e projeto —, em lista aberta de BCP-47 e nunca no `users.locale`
        • ADR 0179 — O laço pergunta ao Jev qual ferramenta, e o modelo do usuário vê só o menu que sobra
        • ADR 0183 — A sessão encerrada pode ser reaberta: uma saída própria dos terminais, para `active`, com evento novo
        • ADR 0184 — Reabrir sessão: as três decisões do dono, e `developer` reabre
        • ADR 0191 — A mensagem que chega com turno em curso entra numa fila persistida e é lida no fim do turno
        • ADR 0226 — Diário vivo e retomada dos agentes: o turno interrompido volta uma vez, do plano
        • ADR 0246 — Anexos do chat: bytes no Postgres, referência no evento, visão por capability
        • ADR 0247 — Uma mensagem, vários destinatários
        • ADR 0249 — O raciocínio do agente é gravado e mostrado só a developer ou mais
      • O time de agentes — papéis, áreas e handoffs
        • ADR 0008 — Agente Criativo conversacional no harness + fundação de handoffs
        • ADR 0009 — Agente PO, backlog (epics/stories/tasks) e rastreabilidade
        • ADR 0010 — Agente Arquiteto: ADRs via PR real, module_map e validação cruzada
        • ADR 0014 — InfraAgent e painel do time ao vivo (fechamento da Fase 4a)
        • ADR 0015 — Psicólogo real: ToolLoop, hipóteses com evidência e triagem de custo
        • ADR 0016 — Anamnese: perfil de proficiência, patches de instrução e loop fechado
        • ADR 0022 — Fechamento da Fase 4b (sessão 1): evidência que chega, custo que aparece, análise que sobrevive ao kill
        • ADR 0023 — Fechamento da Fase 4b (sessão 2): catálogo que funciona, dedup que distingue quem negou, e fila que sobrevive à rodada
        • ADR 0038 — Hierarquia de agentes: áreas, leads e delegação
        • ADR 0046 — Promoção de história com autoridade do usuário
        • ADR 0053 — Dev Lead como área, e paralelismo autorizado pelo usuário
        • ADR 0068 — Diagrama C4 do Arquiteto
        • ADR 0069 — Fusão condicional do aceite do handoff com a ativação de execução
        • ADR 0085 — Fluxo de papéis como registro declarativo (fluxo.yml)
        • ADR 0086 — O plano do Dev Lead suspende o turno para aprovação
        • ADR 0087 — O UX Designer entra como agente conversacional
        • ADR 0088 — Staff: código pronto, dormente para disparo automático
        • ADR 0089 — `analytics` e `delivery-metricas` viram script de relatório
        • ADR 0092 — `platform` nasce como script de relatório sob demanda, não agente
        • ADR 0093 — O papel `dbre` vira dois scripts: linter de migration e relatório de backup
        • ADR 0094 — A delegação Dev Lead → dev vira dado, com `parecerArtifactId` redefinido
        • ADR 0109 — Handoff manual a um agente à escolha reusa oferta+aceite, nunca um terceiro estado de handoff
        • ADR 0182 — A oferta de handoff ganha ciclo de vida: uma pendente por destino, e `superseded`
        • ADR 0185 — "Estou pronto — a necessidade está validada": um clique fecha a prontidão, o gate `necessidade-validada` e o aceite do PO
        • ADR 0186 — O handoff do PO ao Arquiteto é aceito sem clique quando o backlog está coberto e o repositório é local
        • ADR 0194 — Aprovar o plano do Dev Lead ativa a execução, e a tarefa ganha o módulo que ele atribui
        • ADR 0212 — A história arquivada: uma coluna, não um estado da máquina
        • ADR 0213 — O entregável roda na máquina do usuário pelo compose da Infra
        • ADR 0259 — O módulo de que outros dependem tem dono no plano de execução
        • ADR 0261 — O Dev Lead é avisado de bloqueios e pede ao Arquiteto por um canal próprio
      • Gates, QA e revisão de segurança
        • ADR 0013 — Gates de PR: QAAgent e SecOpsAgent
        • ADR 0020 — Destravar os gates de QA e SecOps: gitleaks na árvore, pareceres validados e tool call em texto
        • ADR 0021 — Fechamento da Fase 4a: gate de infra que valida, e painel que diz a verdade
        • ADR 0054 — Gates como registro declarativo
        • ADR 0057 — O agente de gate espera a aprovação, como o dev espera
        • ADR 0067 — O gate sobrevive ao restart
        • ADR 0090 — QA-estratégia e AppSec como segundo MOMENTO, não agente novo
        • ADR 0091 — `secops-runtime` como script de relatório sobre `rate_limit_hits`
        • ADR 0095 — O gate `necessidade-validada` se fecha com confirmação humana separada, não com o Criativo se autovalidando
        • ADR 0123 — Golden-set de casos de regressão para o julgamento semântico do agente de QA Automação
        • ADR 0168 — O golden-set do QA passa a rodar em CI, agendado e semanal
        • ADR 0192 — O plano de teste nasce depois da entrega do dev, e o gate `implementavel` passa a se julgar sobre a história e o `module_map`
        • ADR 0207 — Ciclo de gate parado há mais de 2 h estaciona, e só gesto humano o retoma
        • ADR 0214 — Pergunta em aberto vira pendência a resolver
        • ADR 0215 — A decisão do projeto chega ao dev e ao QA
        • ADR 0258 — A resposta dada pelo chat fecha a pergunta em aberto
      • Execução, política de terminal e aprovações
        • ADR 0011 — Infraestrutura dos dev agents: worktrees, executores git e trava de merge
        • ADR 0012 — DevAgent real: ToolLoop, orçamento por task e disciplina de bloqueio
        • ADR 0017 — Lock de inicialização do workspace, Monitor de dev agents e herança de teto
        • ADR 0018 — NoopDevAgent como modo de execução permanente
        • ADR 0019 — Destravar o DevAgent real: escrita, terminal, ADR por módulo e orçamento por projeto
        • ADR 0045 — Reagendamento por evento do dev agent, e o circuit breaker
        • ADR 0047 — Operabilidade pós-dogfooding: o fechamento da Fase 12
        • ADR 0048 — A decisão no event log, e a ordem do gate
        • ADR 0052 — O dev agent espera a aprovação no meio do laço
        • ADR 0055 — Escopo de caminho na política de terminal
        • ADR 0102 — Revisão do ADR 0065: a fronteira de efeito externo deixa de ser `deny` e vira teto absoluto
        • ADR 0167 — O modo automático libera o escopo de caminho, e só ele
        • ADR 0189 — O piloto automático: o modo automático aprova tudo, menos os tetos, o escopo compara com a pasta real de execução, e "Sempre permitir" grava verbo + subcomando
        • ADR 0204 — O modo automático libera push e PR
      • Onde o código mora — modos de execução
        • ADR 0066 — Nome de pasta legível do workspace
        • ADR 0072 — O projeto escolhe onde o código mora: pasta do usuário ou pasta gerenciada
        • ADR 0104 — `execution_mode` em três valores, e o workspace nasce `unverified` quando o runner é quem verifica
        • ADR 0107 — Navegação de pasta local via o Runner
        • ADR 0108 — No modo `runner`, o projeto nasce ao clicar em "Navegar pasta...", não só na confirmação
        • ADR 0111 — Converter o `execution_mode` de um projeto existente: recusar com dev agent ativo, realocar o `permissions.json`, aposentar a linha do container
        • ADR 0141 — A base única dos projetos montados, montada por identidade
        • ADR 0142 — A validação do workspace montado é adiada até a materialização
        • ADR 0146 — A base consentida no bootstrap, e `mounted` como padrão local
      • Container do projeto e broker
        • ADR 0065 — Container por projeto: a fronteira deixa de ser política
        • ADR 0081 — Ciclo de vida do container: tabela de estado, sem orquestrador
        • ADR 0083 — A aba Terminal ganha o consumidor real do ciclo de vida, não o terminal
        • ADR 0128 — A porta de Docker, e a prova de empacotamento que escolheu a implementação
        • ADR 0130 — O broker de container: quem fala com o Docker do servidor, e por que ele não aceita especificação
        • ADR 0131 — O Arquiteto roteia módulos para infra: ele CANDIDATA, a Infra ELEGE
        • ADR 0133 — A Infra elege entre as candidatas do roteamento, e a eleição vira nova versão de `artifact.project_image`
        • ADR 0134 — Dev agents executam DENTRO do container real do projeto, e o terminal ganha um piso de auto-aprovação lá dentro
        • ADR 0135 — O portão da imagem do Arquiteto (RN-105) passa a valer nos três modos de execução
        • ADR 0136 — Página global de containers: teto por carga no broker, e `container_remove` no teto absoluto
        • ADR 0137 — O `brabo-runner` sobe o container do projeto na máquina do usuário
        • ADR 0143 — Agentes de dev só depois do container
        • ADR 0144 — A segunda raiz do broker, e o localizador discriminado que a escolhe
        • ADR 0180 — O container do projeto roda com o dono da pasta, medido pela api
        • ADR 0190 — A Infra sobe o container no aceite do handoff, por passo do servidor e com a autonomia semeada, e é ela quem entrega ao Dev Lead
        • ADR 0199 — O recurso mínimo do container é derivado do `module_map`: cada módulo declara o seu, e a Infra sobe com a soma
        • ADR 0208 — A Infra herda a rede que o Arquiteto declarou
        • ADR 0228 — O container do projeto sobe o app pelo comando declarado e publica porta só no loopback
        • ADR 0229 — O QA testa numa instância efêmera, com os serviços que a Infra declarou
      • O runner local
        • ADR 0103 — Runner local: execução na máquina do usuário por canal Phoenix com ticket de uso único
        • ADR 0105 — Personal Access Token (`brb_…`) pro runner, escopado por construção a uma rota
        • ADR 0106 — Distribuição do `@brabo/runner` via `tsup` e `npm publish`, versão injetada só em CI
        • ADR 0112 — Binário standalone do `@brabo/runner` via `bun build --compile`, com o addon nativo `.node` embutido por extração real para um diretório temporário
        • ADR 0118 — Configuração do runner pelo navegador: uma chave de dispositivo Ed25519 como segunda credencial do `runner-ticket`, um proxy do GitHub Releases para o binário e a File System Access API para uma pasta configurada com um clique
        • ADR 0145 — Docker vira pré-requisito real do modo `runner`
        • ADR 0147 — Um agente local, capacidades declaradas na conexão
        • ADR 0151 — A base consentida no runner, e a criação de pasta pelo canal
        • ADR 0154 — A chave de dispositivo de máquina
        • ADR 0174 — O binário standalone do runner deixa de prometer o Mac Intel
        • ADR 0187 — O binário standalone do runner deixa de prometer o Windows
        • ADR 0193 — O git credenciado roda no HOST do runner; o código roda no container
        • ADR 0201 — A revogação mira a CHAVE, e não o par `{projeto, usuário}`
        • ADR 0203 — O fluxo do runner pelo navegador é aposentado, e o caminho é o instalador
        • ADR 0230 — O runner importa a chave de API de LLM que o usuário já tem na máquina
      • Autenticação, acesso e segredos

        • ADR 0050 — Credencial sempre cifrada; verificação vira ação explícita
        • ADR 0058 — CSP fechado na api, e o escopo de projeto contido na raiz

        • ADR 0084 — Login social (GitHub/GitLab), revisando o backlog dos ADRs 0031/0032

        • ADR 0127 — Dois tetos de rebaixamento em `project_members`, e a sobreposição fica
        • ADR 0156 — O teto de auto-rebaixamento também na remoção de membro de projeto

        • ADR 0173 — A remoção de membro de workspace nasce, protegida pela mesma cláusula
        • ADR 0245 — Acesso rápido por papel só no ambiente local
      • Providers de LLM, modelos e gasto
        • ADR 0040 — Moeda do dashboard: USD por ora, câmbio manual por workspace adiado
        • ADR 0041 — Uma base OpenAI-compatível, um contrato de LLM providers e capabilities que recusam binding
        • ADR 0042 — Catálogo vivo, ciclo de vida do modelo e preço auditável
        • ADR 0043 — Seis providers de LLM sobre a base, e a Fase 9b finalmente fechada
        • ADR 0049 — Curadoria de modelo por workspace
        • ADR 0051 — Facetas de capability provadas; "para que serve" é curadoria
        • ADR 0063 — Duas audiências para o mesmo gasto: a fatura do owner e o consumo do membro
        • ADR 0064 — Escopo de área na cascata de modelo, e o binding de agente global
        • ADR 0075 — Embeddings no contrato de LLMProvider
        • ADR 0076 — `provider` volta a ser dimensão de gasto, e a contenção passa a ser do tipo
        • ADR 0077 — Ranking de modelos por capacidade, sem nota inventada
        • ADR 0110 — Orçamento por área é ADITIVO, ao lado de projeto/sessão — não uma cascata, e não uma tabela nova
        • ADR 0114 — Detecção de Ollama nativo no bootstrap de dev: perguntar uma vez, persistir no `.env`, pôr os serviços do compose atrás de um profile
        • ADR 0115 — Pull de modelo do Hugging Face: tabela própria, confirmação em dois passos e allowlist de publicadores oficiais — nunca `proposed_actions`
        • ADR 0166 — A preferência de roteamento mora no binding de modelo, viaja com ele, e congela no metering
        • ADR 0188 — O custo real que o provider devolve vira o número do metering
        • ADR 0198 — A duplicata semântica de história e regra é avisada por embedding com limiar, e o gasto entra no metering
        • ADR 0221 — O hub prende a conversa de cada agente ao upstream que a atendeu, por `session_id`
        • ADR 0222 — O 9Router entra como o 10º provider de LLM, com URL por instalação
        • ADR 0223 — O orçamento de raciocínio é capability POR MODELO, curada à mão
        • ADR 0224 — O raciocínio é contado no stream e abortado no limite, e o custo é estimado e reconciliado
        • ADR 0225 — O OpenRouter recebe um teto de preço por chamada (`provider.max_price` = catálogo × 1,5)
        • ADR 0227 — O OpenCode Zen entra como o 11º provider de LLM, só no dialeto OpenAI
        • ADR 0233 — O membro vê o gasto do projeto por agente e por área, nunca por provider
        • ADR 0251 — Preço de lista datado no código para provider que não informa preço
        • ADR 0257 — Na Anthropic direta o raciocínio desligado é por modelo e o esforço reduz o Opus

        • ADR 0113 — Pasta local anexada vira `chunks.scope = 'local'`, lida pelo NAVEGADOR, nunca um caminho do host
        • ADR 0129 — A telemetria de busca do RAG é TABELA, com os pesos congelados na linha
        • ADR 0132 — Golden-set de acerto do RAG, e o gate `rag-acertivo`
        • ADR 0138 — O golden-set do RAG passa a rodar em CI, agendado — e continua `warn`
        • ADR 0148 — Os artefatos dos agentes projetados em arquivo, numa pasta `docs/`
        • ADR 0196 — A Anamnese religada: não roda sem sujeito, e a hipótese aceita vira fato do perfil

    Engine

    PR Gates

    RN-016 — The gate's verdict prevails over the task's statement

    If the task description says one thing and the gate's verdict says another, the verdict wins.

    Since Phase 8b, "the verdict" can come from two specializations — the rule doesn't change: each one prevails over the task WITHIN what it evaluates (Automation over test coverage; Performance/Security over performance NFRs and code findings). QaLead.consolidar/1 doesn't arbitrate between them and the task: any changes_requested from any of them already fails the whole thing (see RN-036).

    • Where: prompt for each specialization in apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/qa_automacao_agent.ex and qa_performance_seguranca_agent.ex
    • Origin: ADR 0020 §9

    Psychologist and Anamnese

    RN-023 — The termination cause is a deterministic classification

    It comes from the recorded reason, never from the LLM's judgment and never by elimination. Every failure records its origin: infra | modelo | código | política.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/psychologist/termination_classifier.ex
    • Origin: ADR 0022 §5 and ADR 0020 §6

    PR Gates

    RN-036 — QA becomes an area: the Lead consolidates without changing the gate's contract

    The Automation specialization (Phase 4a's QAAgent) always delegates; Performance/Security only when the story has a pertinent performance NFR (Engine.Gates.QaLead.rnf_de_performance?/1 — a keyword heuristic, not NLP). The decision NOT to delegate is always recorded — a dispensed delegation with a justification, never silence.

    Consolidation: approved only if ALL delegations that ran approved; any changes_requested fails the whole thing, with itens (items) from the specialization(s) that requested changes, each item prefixed with the label of whoever raised it ("[QA de Automação] ...") — that's how the origin is traced WITHOUT changing itens from string[] to another shape. Delegation failure (any origin — infra, modelo, codigo, politica) NEVER becomes changes_requested: the Lead blocks the task with the real origin (RN-015).

    The qa_verdict that reaches the api is the SAME artifact and goes through the SAME route as always (RecordGateVerdictUseCase, nextGateStatus) — neither of the two changed. What the api learns about the area lives only in the delegation.completed/delegation.failed/ delegation.dispensed events, which the specialization and the Lead record separately.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/qa_lead.ex (consolidar/1, rnf_de_performance?/1), qa_lead_server.ex (the wiring); apps/api/src/application/use-cases/execution/record-delegation.use-case.ts; apps/api/src/db/schema/agents.ts (table delegations, enum failure_origin)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/gates/qa_lead_test.exs (the pure decision tree), qa_lead_server_test.exs (the wiring: decision → delegation → recording → consolidation → the SAME call as always), and — the proof that the contract didn't change — record-gate-verdict.use-case.spec.ts, pr-gate-state-machine.spec.ts and record-infra-gate-verdict.use-case.spec.ts pass with no change at all
    • Origin: ADR 0038

    On the apps/web side (Phase 8d): the team panel groups qa/ qa-automacao/qa-performance-seguranca as lead + collapsible specializations, the PR timeline expands the consolidated verdict into its internals (ProjectApprovalsTab.tsx, PrGateTimeline.tsx), and the feed narrates delegation.* — see apps/web/src/lib/agents.ts (AREAS/areaFor).

    RN-047 — Dev agent circuit breaker: N consecutive blocked stops it, without burning budget in a loop

    Each dev agent keeps a counter (dev_agent_states.consecutive_blocked) of how many tasks ENDED blocked in a row — locally (in the ToolLoop) or remotely (the gate's correction ceiling exhausted). On hitting the per-project ceiling (max_consecutive_blocked, default 3), the agent stops at idle_tripped without trying to claim the next task. An approved terminal outcome resets the counter; an individual blocked task continues the normal flow (returned with a diagnosis, available for a human to unblock) — the breaker is about the SEQUENCE, not the task.

    The only way out of idle_tripped is an explicit rearm (POST .../agents/:agentId/rearm, role developer): it resets the counter and the agent starts trying to claim again. There's no automatic unlock — the same principle as MarkTaskBlockedUseCase/unblock, applied to the sequence instead of the task. Rearming an agent that is not tripped is 409, not silent success: the dev.rearmed event is immutable, and recording it for a rearm that didn't happen would be a lie in the event log.

    A block that comes from OUTSIDE the agent (QaLeadServer failing internally, for example) also needs to wake it — that's why the task.gate_resolved emission lives in MarkTaskBlockedUseCase, the funnel every block passes through, and not in RecordGateVerdictUseCase, which only sees part of them. Without this the agent would stay in awaiting_gate forever, with the task dead and the breaker's counter never incrementing.

    Restarting the engine with an agent in working does not count toward the counter: the retained task is blocked with a restart diagnosis, but that block isn't the agent "burning the ceiling" — it's the infrastructure going down. The counter only rises when the dev↔gate cycle itself produces a real blocked.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/dev/dev_agent_server.ex (finish_task/2, resume_state/2), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/execution/rearm-dev-agent.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/db/schema/iam.ts (projects.max_consecutive_blocked)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/dev/dev_agent_server_test.exs (describe circuit breaker), apps/engine/test/engine/dev/dev_rehydrator_test.exs (describe the four rehydrated states), test/application/use-cases/execution/rearm-dev-agent.use-case.spec.ts
    • Origin: ADR 0045

    RN-050 — With no PR open, no gate opens

    The dev agent proposes commit, push, and PR and reads each one's outcome. It only opens the gate if all three executed. If one stayed pending — the agent's autonomy at require_approval — it enters awaiting_approval, holding the worktree, and opens no gate at all.

    Without this the gate opened regardless, and the damage was silent: QA scans the worktree, not the PR; it found the files, approved; SecOps approved; the task closed as completed — with not one line committed and no PR at all. Only later, when approving the commit, would the user see the action fail (with an empty diagnosis, because System.cmd on a deleted directory returns {"", 2}).

    What releases the agent is task.pr_settled, emitted by the api when pr_open reaches a terminal outcome: opened: true opens the gate; opened: false (denied or failed) returns the task with a diagnosis, instead of leaving the agent waiting forever for a gate no one is going to open.

    A denied PR doesn't count toward the circuit breaker of RN-047: the decision was the user's, not the agent burning the ceiling — the same principle as restart recovery.

    This rule also eliminates D5 (worktree recycled under pending approval) as a consequence: the worktree is only released on gate_resolved, the gate only opens after the PR, and the PR only opens after commit and push.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/dev/agent_io.ex (propose/3), apps/engine/lib/engine/dev/dev_agent_server.ex (abrir_gate/1, aguardar_aprovacao/2), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/actions/execute-git-action.use-case.ts (settlePrOpen)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/dev/dev_agent_server_test.exs (describe pending approval doesn't open a gate)
    • Origin: ADR 0048

    RN-059 — A turn failure is a durable event with an origin, and the agent speaks

    When an LLM turn fails, the agent writes agent.error to the event log with three fields: origem (vocabulary of ADR 0020), mensagem in Portuguese and the raw reason. And the message appears in the conversation thread, not only in the log.

    It used to be the opposite, and the outcome was the worst possible: the four conversational agents wrote agent.response with empty content — indistinguishable from success in the immutable log — and sent the reason through broadcast, which is ephemeral. Whoever did not have the tab open at that second would never know there had been an error; whoever did saw a blank bubble.

    There was a second path, even worse: when the api narrated the failure in the FINAL frame itself (budget, missing credential, missing binding), the turn did not fall into the error branch and emitted no event at all — absolute silence.

    That same branch, once fixed, cost a second round: in the PO, the Architect and the Dev Lead it returned {state, ""} — a TUPLE where all the other branches of run_turn return the state (a map). The Map.put/3 of TurnoAssincrono.tratar_resultado/2 raised BadMapError inside the handle_info, and since the four conversational agents are restart: :temporary, the agent DIED and did not come back. The failure stopped being silent and became a crash — with the most ordinary triggers there are. The rule holds in full: the agent narrates the failure and stays up. That is why tratar_resultado/2 has a second barrier, at the point shared by the four: a turn result that is not a map becomes an agent.error with origin codigo, never a dead process.

    The origin is NEVER guessed: each pattern in FalhaDeTurno.origem/1 has a written reason, and whatever matches none comes out as codigo — the gap is in our classifier, and that is the origin that points to the right action (ADR 0020).

    Events already written are not erased — the screen NAMES them as an empty response prior to this rule, instead of showing blank.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/falha_de_turno.ex, criativo_server.ex, po_server.ex, arquiteto_server.ex, dev_lead_server.ex, infra_lead_server.ex (emit_falha/2), turno_assincrono.ex (tratar_resultado/2, the second barrier), apps/web/src/lib/session-falha.ts
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/criativo_server_test.exs (durable event with an origin; never writes an empty response; an error narrated in the final frame also becomes an event); po_server_test.exs (the final frame with an error does not take the agent down — a REAL GenServer, with Process.alive?/1); arquiteto_server_test.exs and dev_lead_server_test.exs (same path, full cycle); turno_assincrono_test.exs (the second barrier narrates instead of taking it down); apps/web/src/lib/session-falha.test.ts
    • Origin: real execution of PHASE 13b

    RN-061 — A TOOL failure is also an event, and goes back to the model

    A tool call's result is never discarded. It becomes tool.result in the event log (ok, and when it fails, erro), the agent says what happened in the thread, and the reason goes back to the model in the tool role — for it to correct and reemit on the next turn. A tool error is input to the loop, not the end of the line.

    The Creative agent was the only one that discarded it (_ = EmitArtifact.run(args, state)) — the PO and the Architect already fed it back. In a real execution the model emitted titulo/descricao against a schema that requires title/description/origin: the four business rules from the conversation were refused, no event was recorded, and it kept saying "I recorded the rules" with an empty panel.

    The tool's description now NAMES each type's required fields, in English and with a filled-in example — including that business_rule.origin is a non-empty list of seq from the messages that originated the rule, not free text. Without that the model guesses, and guesses in the conversation's language.

    It's the same rule as RN-059 applied to the other failure path: two policies for the same problem would be two chances to swallow the error.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/criativo_server.ex (dispatch_tool, realimentar), apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/emit_artifact.ex (descricao/0), apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/artifact_schemas.ex (required/1)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/criativo_server_test.exs (a refused tool becomes a tool.result with an error, and the agent speaks)
    • Origin: PHASE 13b real execution

    RN-062 — A message to a conversational agent REHYDRATES the process

    A message addressed to the Creative, PO or Architect brings the process up if it is not up, before delivering. The init of each server already rebuilds the history from the event log; what was missing was someone to call it.

    Before, an engine restart killed the conversation silently: the session survived as active, the agent process did not, and the next message died with GenServer.call ... exited — no event, no error on the screen, nothing. The user saw their own message appear and no answer arrive, forever.

    The comment on revise/2 said a dead agent on this route "is a bug". It is — and an engine restart is enough for it to happen. It is the same guarantee that Phase 12b gave the dev agents, applied to the conversational ones.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex (message/2)
    • Test: covered by the agents suite; the execution proof is in docs/explanation/validacao-real.md
    • Origin: real execution of PHASE 13b

    RN-063 — Ending without producing is an outcome, not a failure

    The Anamnese has a tool to say "there is nothing to emit, and this is the reason" (skip_proficiency). The round ends with anamnese.run_skipped and the reason in the payload — never with anamnese.run_failed.

    Before, it did not have that verb. The only tool was emit_proficiency, which refuses an empty list (rightly: an empty profile is not a profile). In a window with no eligible member the Anamnese found that out on the FIRST iteration, wrote in prose "não há membros elegíveis", called emit_proficiency with profiles: [], was refused — and repeated until the iteration ceiling. Each round trip resends the history, which grows with every round trip.

    In a real execution this cost 145 thousand input tokens and 4× the spend of the Creative and the PO combined, producing nothing. And it came back on every tick of the scheduler, every 15 minutes, forever.

    The iteration ceiling worked — it was not an infinite loop. The waste was per round, repeated indefinitely, which is worse: a loop hangs and someone notices; this one bled slowly.

    Narrating run_failed for a round that did the right thing is also a defect: whoever reads the log learns to ignore the failure event.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/anamnese/tools/skip_proficiency.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/anamnese/hooks/termination.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/anamnese_worker.ex (handle_outcome)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/workers/anamnese_worker_test.exs (encerrar sem perfis é DESFECHO: narra run_skipped com o motivo, não falha)
    • Origin: real execution of PHASE 13b

    RN-069 — Retrying a task recreates the branch, doesn't fail

    WorktreeManager.add_worktree/3 uses git worktree add -B (creates or resets), not -b. It already removed the previous worktree's directory, but left the branch behind — and since its name comes from the task's slug, the second attempt on the SAME task always fell into fatal: a branch named 'feature/<slug>' already exists.

    The effect was permanent: unblocking the task didn't help, reactivating execution didn't help, and the circuit breaker disarmed with no way out. In a real execution the only way out was a manual git worktree prune in the project's workspace.

    Resetting is the right call: the previous worktree has already been removed, that attempt's work is worthless (the task went back to the queue), and the branch is reborn from the current point of work_dir.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/dev/worktree_manager.ex
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/dev/worktree_manager_test.exs (retrying the SAME task recreates the worktree instead of failing)
    • Origin: PHASE 13b real execution

    RN-073 — A pending approval SUSPENDS the loop, doesn't burn it

    When a pipeline tool returns pending, the ToolLoop stops and the dev agent enters :awaiting_approval, holding onto the task, the worktree, and the loop's history. The user's decision emits task.action_settled, which wakes it: the real result takes the place where the word "pending" would be, and the loop resumes from where it stopped.

    Two properties the test locks down:

    • Nothing is recorded while waiting. The tool message's slot stays empty. Recording "pending" there would tell the model that's what the command answered — which was exactly the defect.
    • A refusal is a response. The reason takes the result's place and the agent learns that path closed, instead of waiting forever for something no one is going to approve. It's the same principle as pr_settled with opened: false (RN-047), one level down.
    • The wake has to ARRIVE. task.action_settled is born on the task aggregate, not on the proposed_action the table name suggests: the engine's drain reads a closed list of aggregates (session and task). Emitted outside that list, the event is recorded successfully, stays with a null processed_at, and is never even read — no job, no error, no log, and the agent waits forever. The contract crosses two languages, and that's why it's pinned on both sides.

    If the engine restarts during the wait, the rule no longer applies to that task: the suspended loop only exists in memory, so it goes back to the queue blocked with origin infra, and the decision made afterward has nowhere to be applied. Blocking with a diagnosis is deliberate — the alternative was the silent eternal wait, which is exactly what this rule exists to end.

    What this fixes: pending used to come back as the tool's RESULT and the loop kept going. The model read that as the command's answer, learned nothing, tried something else — and each attempt burned an iteration until toolloop.limit_reached {iteration: 8, max_iterations: 8}, with the task blocked for "iteration limit reached" with not one line written. The approvals granted arrived after the loop was exhausted and were useless.

    The terminal allowlist (RN-068) still applies, but stops being the only defense: it's a list of foreseen commands, and the model invents commands.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/hooks/action_pipeline.ex, harness/tool_loop.ex, dev/dev_agent_server.ex, workers/dev_agent_wake_worker.ex; emission in apps/api/src/application/use-cases/actions/{approve,deny}-action.use-case.ts
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/dev/dev_agent_awaiting_approval_test.exs (pending action STOPS the agent; approved: resumes the loop with the REAL output; restart during the wait BLOCKS the task) and apps/engine/test/engine/dev/wake_do_outbox_ao_agente_test.exs, which walks the whole chain — outbox, drain, queue, and process — because the per-link tests were all green with the delivery broken; the aggregate is pinned on the api side in apps/api/test/application/use-cases/actions/approve-deny-action.use-case.spec.ts
    • Origin: ADR 0052, triage phase A

    RN-074 — The terminal output has a byte ceiling

    A command's output is cut at TERMINAL_OUTPUT_MAX_BYTES (default 32 KiB) before becoming the tool result, and the cut leaves a mark stating the two sizes and what to do:

    [saída truncada: 32768 de 1048576 bytes. Refine o comando (head, grep,
-maxdepth) para ver o que falta.]

    Three properties that the test pins down:

    • The ceiling is >, not >=. Output that fits exactly within the limit passes intact — marking it would make the model refine a command that already gave everything.
    • The cut does not split a multibyte character. binary_part/3 cuts by byte; landing in the middle of an é produces invalid binary that breaks the JSON serialization before the result reaches the model.
    • raw_bytes remains the REAL size produced, not the truncated one. It is measurement, and lying in it would hide exactly the behaviour that motivated the ceiling. Whoever wants to detect truncation compares byte_size(stdout) with raw_bytes.

    What this fixes: the output of each command stays in the loop's history and travels in every following turn. Without a ceiling, a find over a large tree is enough — the hello-limpo execution died with {413, "request entity too large"} on turn 18, without a single line written. The overflow is in request bytes, not in context window: the largest successful call had 28,993 input tokens.

    The mark is addressed to the model, not to the human — without saying what to do, it tends to repeat the same command.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/actions/terminal_executor.ex (truncate/2), ceiling in apps/engine/config/runtime.exs
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/actions/terminal_executor_test.exs (describe teto de bytes da saída)
    • Origin: finding S of achados-execucao-real.md, Phase F of the backlog

    RN-076 — The git credential is never written to a file

    The engine works on a remote repository by asking the api for the working remote (GET /internal/projects/:projectId/git-remote), which returns the clean origin and the owner's token separately. The token enters the git invocation through the child process's environment and nowhere else:

    • not in the origin — the clean URL is what stays written in .git/config;
    • not in argv — ps shows the command line of any process;
    • not in a file — neither a persisted helper nor ~/.git-credentials.

    The credential helper is passed through -c, holds only for that process, and comes after an empty credential.helper=: helpers are cumulative and the first to answer wins, so without zeroing first a host helper would answer instead.

    Why this is a rule and not a preference. Writing https://x-access-token:TOKEN@github.com/… into the origin — which almost every tutorial teaches — writes the credential in plain text inside the project folder, exactly where RN-075 gives the dev agent auto-approved read access. A cat .git/config would return the token without going through any approval, and it would travel to the LLM provider in the loop's history. The path scope protects against the agent reading OUTSIDE the project; it cannot protect against a secret the product itself placed INSIDE.

    The credential is the workspace owner's, through the same resolver as RN-058 — two rules for "whose credential is it" would diverge. The local provider has no token and does not query the api: it is resolved straight from the database, and it is the path that pnpm dev and the whole suite exercise.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/actions/git_auth.ex, engine/projects/project_repository.ex (remoto_de_trabalho/1), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/git/get-project-git-remote.use-case.ts
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/actions/git_auth_test.exs (the token does not appear in argv or in the helper) and apps/api/test/application/use-cases/git/get-project-git-remote.use-case.spec.ts (the returned origin contains neither the token nor @)
    • Origin: ADR 0056, finding N, Phase B of the backlog

    RN-077 — The origin of a failure is always one of four

    Every failure outcome names the ORIGIN in the closed vocabulary of ADR 0020 — infra | modelo | codigo | politica. There is no fifth value: null and "indeterminada" are no longer possible.

    Two structural guarantees, and neither depends on anyone remembering:

    • AgentIo.block_task/4 has no default for the origin. It was "mandatory in spirit", with "indeterminada" as the default — and the most expensive outcome of the real execution came out exactly like that, because the call site passed nothing. With no default, forgetting becomes a compile error.
    • FalhaDeTurno.origem/1 always returns one of the four, and there is a table test that fails if any entry — including a never-seen form — produces anything else.

    Why indeterminada was removed. It existed with a reasonable argument: not guessing would be more honest than choosing in the dark. The real effect was the opposite — indeterminada points to no action at all, and whoever triaged the next round started the investigation over from scratch. What it actually meant was the classifier did not recognize this form, which is a gap in our code: codigo is the origin that points to the right action (add the missing clause). The diagnosis still goes verbatim, so nothing is lost.

    The origins are not a guess. Each ToolLoop outcome says who decided to stop: report_blocked and the iteration ceiling belong to the model (it decided, or spent what it had); budget and an unapproved PR are policy (it was a decision, nothing broke); restart and failure to assemble context are infra; and when there is a last_error, the origin comes out of the SAME error the diagnosis narrates — it was that pair that contradicted itself, with diagnosis saying {413, …} and origem saying "indeterminada" on the same line.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/falha_de_turno.ex, engine/dev/agent_io.ex (block_task/4, no default), engine/dev/dev_agent_server.ex (origem_da_parada/1)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/falha_de_turno_test.exs (o vocabulário é fechado) and apps/engine/test/engine/dev/dev_agent_server_test.exs, which asserts the origin in the emitted event
    • Origin: findings P, Q and T of achados-execucao-real.md, Phase G of the backlog

    RN-079 — The Psychologist does not analyze a session with no analyzable event

    Before spending a model turn, the analysis asks whether there is anything to analyze. If there is not, it does not run and the outcome becomes psychologist.analysis_skipped.

    Analyzable excludes two things, for different reasons:

    • the trace of the analysts themselves. The Psychologist writes its turn into the log of the session it is analyzing (agent.response, tool.call, tool.result, the hypothesis). Counting that would make an empty session look populated from the first analysis on, and each retry would fill it more — the criterion would never fail again. It holds the same for the Anamnese;
    • bootstrap.*, which is repository provisioning running on its own: nine machine steps say nothing about the person.

    Everything else counts, including proposed_action.* — the user approving and denying without writing any message is behaviour, a lesson the Anamnese had already learned (RN-063).

    There are two counts, and they do not replace each other. The raw one sizes the work (how much log to read, hence which triage tier, light or heavy); the analyzable one decides whether there is work. Confusing them is the defect: a bootstrap-only session passed as "20 events" without having any, got the analysis, and the model — with nothing to cite — invented nonexistent seq values until the evidence validation rejected it and it gave up, with the budget already spent.

    Skipping holds for manual reprocessing too. Reprocessing does not fabricate material: whoever clicked gets the reason in the log instead of a hypothesis invented about a log that does not exist.

    The skip becomes an event, unlike the Anamnese's, which is only a log — that one runs every 15 min and would become noise, this one runs once per session close, and an absent analysis with nothing narrated is undiagnosable.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/session_events/event.ex (count_analisaveis/1), apps/engine/lib/engine/psychologist/triage.ex (should_run?/1), apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/psychologist_worker.ex
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/session_events/event_analisaveis_test.exs (includes the reproduction of the finding's session: 14 events, none analyzable), apps/engine/test/engine/workers/psychologist_worker_test.exs
    • Origin: finding J, Phase E of the backlog

    RN-080 — A duplicate business rule is refused at entry

    A business_rule whose title already exists in the project is not written. The refusal goes back to the model through the same path as an invalid payload, and it moves on to the next rule instead of stopping.

    At entry because there is nowhere else. There is no rules table: the artifact IS the artifact.business_rule event, and a domain event is neither deleted nor edited. Letting it in means living with the duplicate forever.

    Project scope, not session scope — the duplicate is born across sessions. Running the Creative again opens a new session, and a per-session check would not see the previous round, which is exactly the case of the finding.

    The comparison normalizes case, accents and redundant whitespace; punctuation stays. A semantic duplicate still passes, and that is declared, not forgotten: "Saudação com nome" and "Quem chama pode se identificar" remain two rules, because separating them is judgement and does not fit in an if. Since RN-681 a semantic duplicate of the TITLE is warned about by embedding after writing — it still passes, and is never refused here.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/artifact_dedupe.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/emit_artifact.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/session_events/event.ex (titulos_de_regras/1)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/artifact_dedupe_test.exs, apps/engine/test/engine/harness/emit_artifact_dedupe_test.exs
    • Origin: finding K, Phase E of the backlog

    RN-085 — The iteration ceiling is per agent TYPE

    How many round trips an agent may take in the tool loop depends on the work it does: 8 for those that converse, 60 for the dev agent and for the QA subagents. There is no longer a single number.

    The ceiling of 8 was born from a conversational agent and was inherited by those who work. In the real validation of 13b this showed up as a block: the dev agent spent all eight round trips exploring a freshly provisioned repository and never wrote a file; with 25, it wrote three and ran the tests. The recorded outcome was limite de iterações atingido with origin modelo — technically true and practically useless, because the model never got to judge anything.

    Raising the global default would be the wrong fix, and that is what the rule protects: the Creative does not need 60 round trips to converse, and the ceiling is also the guard against an infinite loop.

    Whoever may go higher is not "whoever works a lot", it is whoever has a spend guard underneath. The iteration ceiling protects against an infinite loop; what protects the POCKET is token_budget_micros. Dev agents and QA subagents run with the task's task_budget_micros, so loosening the round trips does not loosen the bill. infra-workflows uses a heavy tool and still stays at 8: it runs without a budget, and for it the ceiling is the only guard there is.

    Two edges with tests of their own:

    • dev-lead is conversational, despite the dev- prefix that identifies the dev agents (dev-<modulo>, dev-<modulo>-2). The lead decides and delegates, and without the explicit clause it would be born with the heavy-work ceiling by an accident of naming.

    • An unknown agent falls to the lowest ceiling. Err on the cheap side: whoever needs more round trips shows up as limite de iterações atingido and is fixed; whoever gets 60 by mistake spends silently.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/iteracoes.ex, applied in apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tool_loop.ex (init/1)

    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/iteracoes_test.exs and apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tool_loop_test.exs (o teto vem do TIPO do agente quando o chamador não passa um, teto explícito do chamador VENCE o do tipo)

    • Origin: finding X, real validation of 13b; ADR 0053, PHASE 14d

    RN-115 — The Anamnese can be paused globally; the pause belongs to the PRODUCT, it never deletes data

    ANAMNESE_ENABLED (engine env var, boolean, default false as of this rule) decides whether a NEW Anamnese round can happen — periodic (AnamneseSchedulerWorker) or on demand (AnamneseCommandController). A PRODUCT decision by the user on 2026-08-10 ("today it is not bringing very valuable data"), not a bug — see docs/explanation/backlog.md. Disabled, NO existing data is touched: hypotheses, proficiency profiles and instruction patches already stored remain intact and visible, and the PER-MEMBER opt-out (RN-025) remains a separate concept — the pause belongs to the SYSTEM, not to individual profiling.

    AnamneseSchedulerWorker.kickoff/0 (called once at boot) does NOT schedule the periodic job when disabled, instead of scheduling it and letting perform/1 no-op on every tick — cheaper (the Oban queue does not receive a job every ANAMNESE_INTERVAL_SECONDS just to do nothing) and clearer for whoever inspects the queue. Correction on 2026-08-10 (a real finding in execution, not a hypothesis): the original version of this rule left perform/1 unconditional on purpose, so the chain between rounds would not carry the decision to turn on/off with it — but that meant a chain already scheduled BEFORE the flag existed (or before someone disabled it) kept rescheduling itself forever, running the Anamnese for real with the flag saying false. That is exactly what happened on a dev Postgres older than the original PR, remedied by hand by cancelling the scheduled jobs. perform/1 now checks enabled?/0 on every tick, like kickoff/0: if disabled, neither enqueue_projects/0 nor the rescheduling happens, and the chain dies there — which also SELF-HEALS the scenario of an old job that still fires once, without needing manual intervention.

    AnamneseCommandController.run/2 (on-demand route, "reanalisar agora" in Settings) answers 503 with body {"error": "anamnese_desativada"} when disabled — deliberately distinct from the empty 409 that already existed for "project without session" (the two were easy to confuse in a bare 409, and they are quite different causes). RunAnamneseUseCase, on the api side, converts the engine's 503 into ServiceUnavailableException with reason: "anamnese_disabled" in the body — never a generic 500 nor a reused 409. The web (ProjectSettingsTab.tsx) discovers the state on the first click of "Rodar agora" (there is currently no prior read of the global state) and, from then on, disables the button and keeps the explanation VISIBLE on screen — not just a toast that disappears (RN-088: never a silent or confusing failure).

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/anamnese_scheduler_worker.ex (enabled?/0, kickoff/0), apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/anamnese_command_controller.ex, apps/engine/config/runtime.exs, apps/api/src/domain/anamnese/anamnese-disabled.error.ts, apps/api/src/infrastructure/http-clients/api-to-engine-client.ts (runAnamnese), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/anamnese/run-anamnese.use-case.ts, apps/web/src/routes/settings/ProficiencySection.tsx
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/workers/anamnese_scheduler_worker_test.exs (kickoff/0 does not schedule when disabled, schedules when enabled, default off; perform/1 disabled in the middle of the chain does no fan-out nor reschedules), apps/engine/test/engine_web/controllers/anamnese_command_controller_test.exs (503 distinct from 409, with and without session), apps/api/test/application/use-cases/anamnese/run-anamnese.use-case.spec.ts, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectSettingsTab.test.tsx (ProficiencySection)
    • Edge case: the flag is GLOBAL (all projects/workspaces), not per project — unlike the parallelism ceiling (RN-083) or the model inheritable per area (RN-102), which are per-scope decisions. Turning it back on is ANAMNESE_ENABLED=true and restarting the engine; there is no UI button for it (it is operational, not a project preference).
    • Origin: no ADR — a reversible product decision, not a structural architecture change. See docs/explanation/backlog.md.

    RN-116 — A failure to CREATE a handoff does not take the agent down

    confirm_readiness (Creative → PO) and offer_infra_handoff (Architect → Infra; offer_dev_handoff, Architect → Dev Lead, was removed in RN-672, and the handoff to the Dev Lead that Infra offers follows the same ruler) call the api to create the handoff AFTER the turn has already run — in the Creative's case, after the product_brief is already written to the event log. If that call fails (api down, 5xx, etc.), the handoff does not exist, but that NEVER takes the agent's GenServer down: the failure becomes a durable agent.error, with origem (FalhaDeTurno.origem/1) and a message that says what HAS already been saved (the product_brief, the rules) and what has not (the handoff) — so the user knows that confirming again is safe and does not repeat work.

    It was the opposite: the three calls used {:ok, _handoff} = EngineApiClient.create_handoff(...) — a rigid match. {:error, _} became a MatchError, and since the three agents come up with restart: :temporary in a :one_for_one DynamicSupervisor, the process simply VANISHED — no agent.error, no answer in the thread, only silence. From the observer's side: the information (rules, product brief) seemed to have "gone through" (it was written), but nothing started on the next agent's side, because the handoff never came to exist. Reopening the conversation did not fix it by itself — only a NEW message reactivates the process (rehydrating from the event log), and only a new readiness confirmation tries the handoff again.

    The message does NOT reuse FalhaDeTurno.mensagem/1 (the one from RN-059, "não consegui completar este turno... nada foi gasto"): at the three call sites the work already ran (or did not even need to run, in the case of offer_dev_handoff) — saying "nothing was spent" would be false when tokens had already been spent in the consolidation turn. It reuses only FalhaDeTurno.origem/1, which classifies by the SHAPE of the reason (HTTP status, transport exception), not by being an LLM turn.

    Engine.Harness.Tools.OfferHandoff (the tool the PO uses via a tool call, inside the ToolLoop) already handled {:error, reason} without crashing — the defect was only in these three server-driven handlers, which call EngineApiClient.create_handoff/5 DIRECTLY instead of going through the tool.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/criativo_server.ex (handle_call(:confirm_readiness, ...), emit_falha_handoff/3), apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/arquiteto_server.ex (handle_call(:offer_infra_handoff, ...), emit_falha_handoff/3); apps/engine/lib/engine/infra/infra_lead_server.ex (emit_falha_do_handoff_ao_dev_lead/2, RN-672)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/criativo_server_test.exs ("prontidão: falha ao criar o handoff NÃO derruba o processo, e vira agent.error durável"); apps/engine/test/engine/agents/arquiteto_server_test.exs (offer_infra_handoff, success and failure); apps/engine/test/engine/infra/infra_lead_server_test.exs (the handoff to the Dev Lead that Infra offers, refused with 500)
    • Origin: real-use report in project exp-001 (Creative → PO); the same structural failure was found by reading the code in the Architect's two handoffs, with no separate reproduction for them

    RN-117 — The Psychologist can be paused globally; the pause belongs to the PRODUCT, it never deletes data

    PSYCHOLOGIST_ENABLED (engine env var, boolean, default false) decides whether a NEW Psychologist round can happen — automatic (session closing, routed by Engine.Outbox.Drain) or on demand (PsychologistCommandController.reanalyze/2). The same PRODUCT decision by the user on 2026-08-10 already applied to the Anamnese (RN-115, "today it is not bringing very valuable data") — not a bug, see docs/explanation/backlog.md. Disabled, NO existing data is touched: analyses and hypotheses already emitted remain intact and visible.

    Unlike the Anamnese (whose automatic trigger is a periodic TICK that the flag itself decides whether to reschedule), the Psychologist's automatic trigger is session closing — Engine.Outbox.Drain routes session.closed/session.closed_abnormally to PsychologistWorker only when PsychologistWorker.enabled?/0 is true (Drain.handlers_for/1); disabled, only SessionLifecycleWorker runs, and the Psychologist job is not even born. PsychologistWorker.perform/1 remains unconditional on purpose — but it is NO longer "the same pattern" as AnamneseSchedulerWorker (see the 2026-08-10 correction in RN-115 above): there perform/1 started checking the flag because it ITSELF reschedules the chain on every tick, and an old job firing unconditionally reopened the Anamnese with the flag off. The Psychologist has no chain that reschedules itself — each job is born from ONE event (session.closed), and what decides is the Drain at the moment it RECEIVES the event, not the worker at the moment it RUNS; a Psychologist job already enqueued before turning the flag off is, at most, the last pending round, never an infinite chain. That is why the pre-existing suite of PsychologistWorker (which calls perform/1 directly) did not need to change.

    PsychologistCommandController.reanalyze/2 (on-demand route, "Reanalisar" in the Insights tab) answers 503 with body {"error": "psicologo_desativado"} when disabled, without even creating the job. ReanalyzeSessionUseCase, on the api side, converts the engine's 503 into ServiceUnavailableException with reason: "psychologist_disabled" in the body — never a generic 500. This discovers the pause when there is already an analysis to reprocess; the screen with NO hypothesis at all has its own prior read, which did not exist here — see RN-454.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/psychologist_worker.ex (enabled?/0), apps/engine/lib/engine/outbox/drain.ex (handlers_for/1), apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/psychologist_command_controller.ex, apps/engine/config/runtime.exs, apps/api/src/domain/psychologist/psychologist-disabled.error.ts, apps/api/src/infrastructure/http-clients/api-to-engine-client.ts (reanalyzeSession), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/execution/reanalyze-session.use-case.ts, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectInsightsTab.tsx
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/outbox/drain_test.exs (session.closed only enqueues the Psychologist when enabled), apps/engine/test/engine_web/controllers/psychologist_command_controller_test.exs (503 without creating a job, 202 with the job enqueued when enabled), apps/api/test/application/use-cases/execution/reanalyze-session.use-case.spec.ts, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectInsightsTab.test.tsx
    • Edge case: the flag is GLOBAL (all projects/workspaces), like the Anamnese's. Turning it back on is PSYCHOLOGIST_ENABLED=true and restarting the engine; there is no UI button for it.
    • Origin: no ADR — a reversible product decision, not a structural architecture change. See docs/explanation/backlog.md.

    Session

    RN-122 — The "Stop" button cancels the turn FOR REAL, killing the task holding the LLM call

    Until now there was no cancellation anywhere. The root was structural: agent_command_controller.ex handled the user's message with GenServer.call(pid, {:user_message, texto}, ...) — SYNCHRONOUS — and the entire turn (tool loop + SSE call to the LLM) ran INSIDE handle_call. The agent's process stayed blocked until the turn finished and handled NO other message in the meantime — not even a future cancel command.

    The fix: the turn handle_call/handle_cast of the four conversational agents (Creative, PO, Architect, Dev Lead) spawns a Task.Supervisor.async_nolink/2 (Engine.Agents.TurnoAssincrono, new, shared by all four) with the heavy work and returns {:noreply, state} — the reply to the original GenServer.call is DEFERRED until the task finishes (GenServer.reply/2, fired when {ref, resultado} arrives at handle_info). Since the agent process STOPS being blocked, a new handle_cast(:cancel, state) arrives and is handled while the turn runs: Task.shutdown/2 in :brutal_kill mode kills the task, which drops the HTTP (SSE) connection it holds with the api — that's what makes cancellation actually save tokens, not just stop rendering on the client. The original from receives {:error, :cancelado}, and a TERMINAL agent.error is recorded with origin "politica" (policy — the same origin as budget/credential/binding — cancelling is a user decision to stop spending tokens, not a fifth value in ADR 0020's closed vocabulary) — without it, GetSessionPendingWorkUseCase would see agent.activated with no subsequent agent.response/agent.error, and the session would stay hung on the pending-work signal.

    Two competing messages to the same agent (the user sends again while a turn is already running) never spawn a second task: TurnoAssincrono.iniciar/3 replies {:error, :turno_em_andamento} (turn in progress) right away.

    Revised by RN-578 (ADR 0163): the reply to the original GenServer.call is no longer deferred until the task finishes — it goes out on ACCEPTANCE, as soon as the task is up, and cancelling answers nobody (the caller already has :ok). {:error, :turno_em_andamento} stopped being discarded by the controller: it is a named 409 and a durable agent.error.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/turno_assincrono.ex (the mechanism), apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/{criativo,po,arquiteto,dev_lead}_server.ex (the four turn handle_call/handle_cast), apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex:373 (cancel/2), apps/engine/lib/engine_web/router.ex (POST /internal/sessions/:sessionId/agent/cancel), apps/api/src/application/use-cases/agents/cancel-agent-turn.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/interfaces/http/agents/agents.controller.ts (POST /projects/:projectId/sessions/:sessionId/agents/:agent/cancel), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (handleCancel, "Stop" button in the composer)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/turno_assincrono_test.exs (the task dies FOR REAL — Process.alive?/1 before and after — not just "stopped importing the result"), apps/api/test/application/use-cases/agents/cancel-agent-turn.use-case.spec.ts
    • Edge case: cancelling with no turn in progress is an idempotent NO-OP — there's no task to kill nor pending from to reply to. Cancellation does NOT undo a side effect that already ran BEFORE the task died (e.g., a tool that had already been dispatched and recorded its event) — it only interrupts what hasn't happened yet.
    • Origin: this session's investigation; no dedicated ADR (a concurrency pattern change INSIDE the harness, not a layer/database boundary).

    RN-129 — The ToolLoop never writes an empty agent.response; a transport failure becomes a durable agent.error

    RN-059 closed the empty bubble for the four conversational agents, but they do not go through Engine.Harness.ToolLoop — each one calls EngineApiClient.llm_turn_stream/6 in its own module. The ToolLoop (used by dev agents, QA Automation/Performance-Security, Infra-Workflows, Anamnese and Psychologist) had the SAME defect on a different path: it emitted agent.response on every iteration, even when the model only called a tool without text, or ended the turn without producing anything — and the transport failure (provider down, timeout) became an agent.response with absent content, equally indistinguishable from success.

    Found live in a real execution session (dev agents): two bubbles with the RN-059 compatibility text ("resposta vazia — evento anterior...") appeared in a session created minutes earlier — they were not old events, they were the same defect happening again, only in the execution tab.

    Two fixes, at the STRUCTURAL point common to every ToolLoop consumer, not module by module:

    1. Empty content never becomes agent.response. An iteration that only called a tool is already narrated by tool.call/tool.result; an iteration that produced nothing (neither text nor tool call) leaves the outcome for the loop's caller to decide — ctx.last_error/{:ok, ctx} carry the information, and the consumer (e.g.: DevAgentServer.handle_outcome/4) already writes the durable event of its OWN domain (dev.blocked, with origem).
    2. A transport failure becomes a durable agent.error, with origem (Engine.Agents.FalhaDeTurno.origem/1 — the SAME helper the four conversational agents use, without duplicating classification) and mensagem in Portuguese — never again an agent.response without content.
    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tool_loop.ex (loop/1, emit_falha/2)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tool_loop_test.exs ("iteração só com tool call (sem texto) não grava agent.response vazio", "modelo termina o turno sem texto e sem tool call...", "falha de transporte... grava agent.error durável com origem")
    • Origin: RN-059 (the rule this one extends) — found live in a real execution session with dev agents

    PR Gates

    RN-140 — A gate cycle that dies mid-way resumes on its own, with no manual intervention

    QaLeadServer/SecOpsAgentServer are restart: :temporary, and the gate's intermediate transitions (DevAgentServer.correct/3, Dispatcher.run_secops/2) are direct in-memory calls — made AFTER record_gate_verdict has already durably advanced gate_status on the api. A crash between the two used to trap the PR forever in awaiting_qa/awaiting_secops: nothing knew that step had been left pending, and no engine restart fixed it — ADR 0057 itself already declared suspension in {:awaiting, ...} as a known limit, and investigating again found that the window between a recorded verdict and a dispatch call is, in practice, easier to hit.

    gate_states (schema engine, key {project_id, task_id, gate}) records the in-flight cycle at the SAME points where the transitions already happened — "in_progress" before any subagent/scanner runs, "dispatch_pending" right after record_gate_verdict returns correct/run_secops and before the in-process call. Engine.Gates.GateRescuer scans rows stuck for more than 15 minutes (configurable, GATE_RESCUE_STALE_AFTER_SECONDS) — generous on purpose, because a QA subagent's ToolLoop legitimately runs up to TOOL_LOOP_MAX_ITERATIONS_GATE (60) iterations, and a short threshold would rescue (and duplicate) a cycle that's merely slow — and resumes: "in_progress" restarts the whole area (no surgical resumption of ctx, which doesn't survive a restart, the same choice as the dev agent); "dispatch_pending" resends exactly the lost call. Called on boot and by an Oban tick self-rescheduled every 5 minutes (GATE_RESCUE_INTERVAL_SECONDS), Engine.Workers.GateRescueSchedulerWorker — the same idiom as ModelSyncSchedulerWorker/AnamneseSchedulerWorker.

    Two guards against duplicating work: a process alive ON THE SAME node (Registry.lookup) is never disturbed, and the staleness threshold covers what the local guard doesn't reach (remote replica — Registry is local to the node, the same caveat as Engine.Dev.Wake since ADR 0045). The worst outcome of a residual race is duplicated, cheap work — the api rejects a second record_gate_verdict for a gate that no longer owns gate_status (nextGateStatus), and DevAgentServer.correct/3 is already idempotent via a state guard since ADR 0052 — never inconsistent data.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/gate_state.ex, gate_rescuer.ex, the write points in qa_lead_server.ex (run_area/3, apply_gate_result/6) and secops_agent_server.ex (run_secops/3, apply_verdict/6), and apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/gate_rescue_scheduler_worker.ex
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/gates/gate_rescuer_test.exs — kills a real QaLeadServer with the cycle in flight (DynamicSupervisor.terminate_child/2, the durable row surviving the process) and proves GateRescuer.run/0 reconnects the area on its own up to a real outcome; the scenario in the statement (lost run_secops) and the lost correct return, both with REAL process and dispatch, no FakeGateDispatcher; and the two guards (a live local process isn't disturbed; a recent row isn't touched)
    • Origin: ADR 0067, which extends ADR 0057

    RN-141 — The content read by read_file also has a byte ceiling

    RN-074 locked the terminal output against {413, "request entity too large"}, but left the same door open through read_file: it read the WHOLE file, with no ceiling, and that content entered the loop history and traveled in every following turn. A PR with a large file (lockfile, bundle, generated file) was enough to lock up dev agents AND the Performance/Security QA — which only has ReadFile/SearchWorkspace (no Terminal, on purpose) to investigate a PR, so it had no escape route at all when the file was too large.

    The content is cut at READ_FILE_MAX_BYTES (default 32 KiB, the same value as RN-074 by coincidence of context, not by coupling — the two variables are independent) before becoming the tool result, with a marker stating the file and the two sizes:

    [arquivo package-lock.json truncado: mostrando 32768 de 1048576 bytes. Use
search_workspace para localizar um trecho específico em vez de reler o
arquivo inteiro.]

    The same three properties as RN-074 (the ceiling is > not >=; the cut does not split a multibyte character; the marker is addressed to the model, saying what to do). The truncation lives in the TOOL (Engine.Harness.Tools.ReadFile), not in Engine.Harness.WorkspaceFiles.read_file/2 — that is the generic file access base, shared by write_file/search_workspace, and truncating there would cut content of those who do not need that ceiling.

    search_workspace did not get the same treatment: it returns only the PATHS that matched (matched_content is a boolean), never the file content — the overflow vector that motivated this RN does not apply to it.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/read_file.ex (truncate/2), ceiling in apps/engine/config/runtime.exs (read_file_max_bytes)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tools/read_file_test.exs (describe teto de bytes do conteúdo)
    • Origin: found live in the event log of a real execution — the 4 dev agents of a project and the Automation/Performance-Security QAs blocked with {413, "request entity too large"}, the same root cause as RN-074, a different door

    Session

    RN-142 — Confirming readiness with NO business rule at all is refused by the engine, not just hidden in the UI

    Before, clicking "I'm ready to produce" — or calling the route directly — ALWAYS created the product_brief and offered the handoff to the PO, even in a conversation where zero business rules had been captured. business_rule_refs(state) already existed in criativo_server.ex, but only to POPULATE the brief's "rules" field — never as a blocking condition. In the UI, SessionPage.tsx disabled the button only during streaming, without checking the rule count.

    The real guarantee had to live on the server, not on the screen: whoever calls the route directly (or a future client that isn't this frontend) can't punch through the guardrail just because the button is drawn disabled. CriativoServer.handle_call(:confirm_readiness, ...) now checks business_rule_refs(state) BEFORE spawning the Engine.Agents.TurnoAssincrono Task (RN-122) — empty, it refuses right there, without running the consolidation turn, without a product_brief, without a handoff.

    The refusal doesn't turn into an HTTP 4xx: agent_command_controller.ex#readiness/2 IGNORES this GenServer.call's return and always answers 202, the same pattern message/2 already uses since RN-122 ("this response is just the acknowledgment" — the real outcome lives in the event log). That's why the refusal is narrated as a DURABLE agent.error (RN-059), with origin "politica" (policy — it's a product decision, not an infra/model/turn failure) — and a message explaining why; the session thread already knows how to render this event type (lerFalhaDeTurno, the same red bubble as any other turn failure).

    The complementary UX, in SessionPage.tsx: the button is born disabled (with a title explaining why) while events has no artifact.business_rule — the SAME source that already feeds the "Business rules" panel (ContextAside), with no second read that could diverge from the first.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/criativo_server.ex (handle_call(:confirm_readiness, ...), emit_falha_sem_regra/1), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (hasBusinessRule, the "I'm ready to produce" button)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/criativo_server_test.exs ("readiness: refuses when NO business rule was captured" — no turn, no brief, no handoff; the refusal narrated with origin "politica"), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.readiness-exige-regra.test.tsx (button disabled with no rule, enabled with 1+)
    • Edge case: the session already had readiness.confirmed recorded by the api BEFORE signaling the engine (ConfirmReadinessUseCase) — that doesn't change: it's the record of the user's CLICK, a fact that happened independent of the engine accepting or refusing afterward.
    • Origin: code investigation confirming a user report — no ADR (new guardrail on an already-existing flow, no layer/database boundary change).

    RN-150 — search_workspace has a ceiling on hit COUNT and on BYTES, each with its own marker

    Found in a PR review: search_workspace (dev agents and the two QA/gate agents, qa_tools.ex and qa_performance_seguranca_agent.ex — the latter only has read_file/search_workspace, without Terminal, on purpose) returned ALL search results, with no ceiling at all — the same class as finding S (Engine.Actions.TerminalExecutor.truncate/2) and as the read_file fix (Engine.Harness.Tools.ReadFile.truncate/2): the result stays in the loop history and travels in every following turn, and a large tree is enough to blow the provider's {413, "request entity too large"}.

    Two independent ceilings, because the search overflows in two different ways:

    1. Hit count — a tree with thousands of files matching the term produces thousands of - caminho lines even if no individual file is large. Truncating only by BYTES at the end would still pay the cost of scanning and reading the content of each of those files before building the string. That is why the count ceiling (SEARCH_WORKSPACE_MAX_HITS, default 500) lives in WorkspaceFiles.search/3, which already STOPS consuming the search as soon as it finds enough hits — the pipeline runs over a Stream, and Enum.take(stream, max_hits + 1) only reads from the source what it needs to produce the first max_hits + 1 results. The "+1" is what allows saying there WERE more without continuing to scan the rest to count the exact total — counting the total would pay again the I/O the ceiling exists to avoid, so the marker says "showing the first N" and never invents a total.
    2. Bytes of the final text — even with limited hits, very long paths can produce a large string. Byte ceiling (SEARCH_WORKSPACE_MAX_BYTES, default 32,768), same pattern as terminal_output_max_bytes/read_file_max_bytes — its OWN variable, it does not reuse the other two: same class of overflow, diverging one should not require touching the others.

    The truncation marker is addressed to the MODEL, not to the human: it says what was cut (hits and/or bytes) and instructs to refine the search term — the same spirit as the TerminalExecutor/ReadFile markers.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/workspace_files.ex (search/3, take_capped/2), apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/search_workspace.ex (truncate/3, marca_de_truncagem/5), apps/engine/config/runtime.exs (search_workspace_max_hits, search_workspace_max_bytes)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/workspace_files_test.exs (search/3 with max_hits cuts the COUNT and marks truncation only when there are more than the ceiling), apps/engine/test/engine/harness/search_workspace_test.exs (a search with few results is not altered; a search with more hits than the ceiling is truncated with a clear warning; a final text larger than the byte ceiling is also cut)
    • Origin: PR review finding — the second real cause of the 413 in reviews, after the read_file fix

    RN-162 — The Creative's structured questions

    The Creative can, when it asks SEVERAL questions in the same response, emit the list in a STRUCTURED format instead of letting the user answer item by item in free text — a new tool ask_structured_questions (apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/ask_structured_questions.ex, :direct), registered next to emit_artifact. Schema: { questions: [{ id, label, type?, options? }] } — id unique and non-empty, label non-empty, type ∈ text|textarea|select (default text), options mandatory and non-empty when type: select. It writes chat.structured_question.

    The frontend (StructuredQuestionCard, SessionPage.tsx) renders a form with one field per question — Input/Textarea/Select from the design system, according to type. POST .../agents/:agent/structured- question/:questionSetId/answer (AnswerStructuredQuestionUseCase) validates that every question has a non-empty answer, writes chat.structured_question_answered (referencing questionSetId = the id of the question event) and REUSES SendAgentMessageUseCase — the answers become a concatenated message ("1. {label}: {resposta}\n2. ..."), as if the user had typed in the thread; there is no new "the agent reads the structured answer" channel. A set of questions can only be answered ONCE: resubmission is refused with 409, and the form does not even reappear — the card becomes read-only as soon as there is a later chat.structured_question_answered with the same questionSetId.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/ask_structured_questions.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/criativo_server.ex, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/agents/answer-structured-question.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/interfaces/http/agents/agents.controller.ts, apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (StructuredQuestionCard)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tools/ask_structured_questions_test.exs, apps/engine/test/engine/agents/criativo_server_test.exs, apps/api/test/application/use-cases/agents/answer-structured-question.use-case.spec.ts, apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.perguntas-estruturadas.test.tsx
    • Origin: user request — no precedent of structured input in the chat

    RN-163 — The Creative keeps the promise to try again

    Each Creative turn runs a bounded tool-use loop, with a ceiling of 12 trips to the model — the same design the PO and the Architect already had. Before, the model was called ONCE per turn: the tool result was appended to the in-memory history and nobody read it again, so the sentence "vou corrigir e tentar de novo" — literal in the code — was only fulfilled if the user sent another message. For whoever used it, the Creative simply stopped answering after saying it was going to fix it.

    Four consequences, and each one is a rule:

    1. A tool error is input, not the end of the line. The reason goes back to the model as a tool message and the loop calls the model again, which re-emits it corrected WITHIN the same turn.
    2. Nothing is announced that the code will not execute. The retry sentence is decided after dispatching the tools and knowing how many rounds are left: with a round available, the agent says it will fix it; without a round, it does not promise.
    3. The tool failure became a durable agent.error, with origin (RN-059) — it was an agent.response, indistinguishable in the event log from a normal response. origem: infra when the api refused the append_event; origem: modelo for the payload the model wrote (wrong key, origin that is not a list, duplicated rule, system-emitted type). The payload carries tool and retentativa.
