    4. An exhausted ceiling does not end in silence. It becomes an agent.error with reason: "limite_de_iteracoes" and origem: modelo, the same reading as the toolloop.limit_reached's ToolLoop. The event name is NOT reused: this agent does not run inside the ToolLoop, and the event would lie about who produced it.

    Two boundaries of the loop, both deliberate: a successful ask_structured_questions ends the turn (the ball is with the user, and the answers come back in a future turn — RN-162); and a turn that had a failure in some round closes with a CONSOLIDATED outcome in the thread, so the last word is not the error of a round that was already corrected later.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/criativo_server.ex (run_turn_capturing/3, continuar/4, emit_falha_de_ferramenta/4, emit_falha_limite/2)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/criativo_server_test.exs (a loop that really corrects, an exhausted ceiling narrated, origin infra vs modelo, a structured question that ends the turn)
    • Origin: real use in the exp001 project — "the Creative did not answer after saying it would fix it and try again"

    RN-164 — The PO READS what already exists, scoped to the project

    The PO gained two READ tools — listar_regras_de_negocio and listar_backlog (:direct, with no parameter at all) — served by two internal routes scoped to the project: GET /internal/projects/:projectId/business-rules and GET /internal/projects/:projectId/backlog.

    The first returns every artifact.business_rule of the project's sessions with the whole description, which stories already cite each rule and the uncoveredCount; the second returns the SAME epic → story → task tree as the Backlog tab, through the same ListBacklogUseCase (three reads per project, never N+1). The text returned to the model puts the UNCOVERED rules first and the ORPHAN epics before the tree — that is what generates work.

    What the rule fixes: until now the PO had four tools, all of them write tools. Its context was built once, at kickoff, from the 200 last events of the session — from then on it did not know which rules existed, which it had already covered, nor what it had already created. The scope is the project and not the session on purpose: a rule captured in a previous session is exactly what the per-session read hid.

    Reading does not become a proposed_action (it is not an external effect), but it is CONTAINED in the sense of ADR 0060: neither of the two routes accepts a parameter beyond the project id — no search, no pagination, no filter —, the cost per call is constant, and the text delivered to the model has a line ceiling stating the real total when it truncates.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/listar_regras_de_negocio.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/listar_backlog.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/po_server.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/engine_api_client.ex, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/backlog/list-business-rules.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/interfaces/http/internal/internal-projects.controller.ts
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tools/listar_regras_de_negocio_test.exs, apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tools/listar_backlog_test.exs, apps/engine/test/engine/agents/po_server_test.exs, apps/api/test/application/use-cases/backlog/list-business-rules.use-case.spec.ts
    • Origin: real use in the exp001 project — "create a tool so the PO can list the business rules"

    RN-165 — An epic without a story is called out, and the PO asks when it does not know

    When the PO ends a turn having created an epic and no story for it, that becomes an EXPLICIT outcome: a durable event backlog.epic_without_story (with origem: "modelo", the ids and titles of the epics and the message) plus the agent.error broadcast — the pattern of RN-059: the log is what survives, the broadcast is the agent saying it in the thread. The call-out is per OCCURRENCE: once reported, the pending list is emptied, and it does not become an alarm that repeats on every turn.

    What counts is the creation that succeeded: a create_story refused by the api (nonexistent business_rule_id, for example) does not settle the epic. Treating it as if it did would be trading one silence for another. The pending epics live in the PoServer state and are NOT rehydrated: the call-out is about the obligation assumed in the turn, and rebuilding it from the event log would reopen an old epic the user has already resolved some other way.

    Along with it came the two pieces that were missing for the PO to have a way out other than stopping: ask_structured_questions — the SAME tool as the Creative's (RN-162), just advertised in po_server — and the kickoff instruction saying, in so many words, that an epic without a story blocks execution and that when information is missing, one ASKS, never stops nor invents. The previous instruction did not say a word about what to do in the face of a gap, and in the face of a gap without instruction a model chooses between inventing and stopping.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/po_server.ex (anotar_obrigacao/4, encerrar_turno/1, obrigacoes/0), apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/create_epic.ex (id_no_resultado/1), apps/web/src/lib/activity.ts
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/po_server_test.exs (describe "RN-165"), apps/web/src/lib/activity.test.ts
    • Origin: real use in the exp001 project — a backlog without stories, hence without tasks, hence execution stuck without a visible error

    RN-166 — The PO's iteration ceiling leaves a trace

    Once the iteration ceiling of the PoServer tool loop (12) is exhausted, the turn emits toolloop.limit_reached with {iteration, max_iterations} — the SAME type and the same payload that Engine.Harness.ToolLoop already emitted since Fase 3. Before, the clause returned the state and that was it: from outside, an exhausted loop was indistinguishable from a turn that simply ended.

    Reusing the identifier instead of creating a po.* is deliberate: it is the same fact (the loop hit the ceiling), and whoever reads the event log should not need to learn a second name because the conversational agent has its own loop instead of using the ToolLoop.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/po_server.ex (run_turn/2, clause remaining <= 0)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/po_server_test.exs ("teto de iterações emite toolloop.limit_reached")
    • Origin: investigation of the exp001 hang — the loop ended silently

    RN-171 — The list question has a free-text way out, by default

    A type: "select" question of ask_structured_questions (RN-162) accepts an answer outside the list. The field is allowOther, and the default is true: whoever declares nothing offers the way out. Closing the list requires an explicit allowOther: false, and only makes sense when it is genuinely closed ("Sim"/"Não").

    The open default is not a style preference. A list closed by the model's OVERSIGHT locks the whole conversation and the user has no way to unlock it from outside — that is exactly what real use found: the model offered an option of the kind "Escreva você mesmo" and the form had nowhere to write, because the tool schema did not know how to express "besides these, whatever you want". A list opened by mistake, in the worst case, offers one field too many. The two errors do not cost the same. For the same reason the tool description forbids creating an "Outro" option inside options: the form already offers it by itself, and two escape hatches in the same list confuse.

    allowOther only exists in select — in text/textarea the field is already free text, and the engine normalizes these two to false instead of writing a meaningless state in the event log. On screen, choosing "Outra (escrever)" swaps the Select for an Input: the interface sentinel (__outra__) never travels to the backend, and what goes is the typed TEXT. The submit button keeps requiring ALL questions filled — AnswerStructuredQuestion UseCase refuses with 400 listing what is missing, so enabling it with an empty field would only produce a server error —, and being on "Outra" with the text still blank does NOT count as filled.

    The card also stopped being the only item in the thread aligned to nothing: it is now centered with the same 560px ceiling as the ApprovalCard in the chat variant (RN-173) and gets the agent's avatar and color, which is what makes it read as SOMEONE's utterance instead of an orphan form. Before, it was born pushed to the left with a 480px ceiling, while the bubbles start 45px inward.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/ask_structured_questions.ex (schema, validation and normalizar/1), apps/web/src/lib/api-types.ts (StructuredQuestion.allowOther), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (StructuredQuestionCard, OUTRA_RESPOSTA, permiteOutra), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.module.css (.structuredQuestionCard, .structuredQuestionCabecalho)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tools/ask_structured_questions_test.exs (open default, explicit false, non-boolean allowOther refused), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.perguntas-estruturadas.test.tsx (describe "saída por texto livre no select")
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "always give the user-input option when they select Write"

    RN-175 — Every agent response says which model it was generated with

    agent.response carries modelName in the three producers of the event, and not only in the four conversational agents:

    producer who goes through there before
    the four conversational ones (criativo/po/arquiteto/dev_lead) chat already wrote it, since RN-146
    Engine.Harness.ToolLoop EVERY execution and gate agent — dev-*, QA, SecOps, Infra-Workflows, Psychologist, Anamnese never wrote it
    SendChatMessageUseCase (api) chat without an active agent, in which the one answering is the model never wrote it

    No new call: RunLlmTurnUseCase already returns modelName in the body and StreamLlmTurnUseCase already puts it in the final frame — the value is the name of the model of the resolved binding (model.name), not the provider's echo. It is null when the turn failed before resolving the binding, and absent in an event written before this rule.

    On screen, the model stopped being the loose word modelo in mono 10px --text-muted next to the agent's name — which reads as if the model were CALLED "modelo", and which fails the reading-text contrast. It became a chip with the model icon, --text-secondary over --surface-2, and the unknown label became "modelo não registrado". The screen does not guess the model from the agent's CURRENT binding when the data is missing: attributing to an old response a model that perhaps did not even exist when it was generated would be inventing provenance, and invented provenance is worse than absent — the same argument as the frozen price in token_usage (RN-044).

    Out of scope, declared: agent.error still lacks the model in the four servers, even when the api sent it in the error frame (budget exceeded, for example). It is a change to another event, with another question to answer.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tool_loop.ex, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/llm/send-chat-message.use-case.ts, apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.tsx (the chip in agent.response), apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.module.css (.messageModelo)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/harness/tool_loop_test.exs, apps/api/test/application/use-cases/llm/send-chat-message.use-case.spec.ts, apps/web/src/routes/SessionPage.arquiteto-modelo-icone.test.tsx, apps/web/src/design-contraste.test.ts (the chip's pair)
    • Origin: real use on exp001 — "the PO did not show the model it was using; all agents must show their model next to their name"

    fluxo.yml × code audit — the Dev Lead's plan becomes a real approval (RN-284, ADR 0086)

    This is not a planned phase: it fixes a divergence that the audit of packages/contracts/fluxo.yml × code found (docs/explanation/auditoria-fluxo-vs-codigo.md, finding A2) — the flow already declared the dev-lead's plano-de-paralelismo output as via: proposed_action, and the code was never adjusted to match.

    RN-284 — A conversational agent's turn can SUSPEND waiting for human approval

    Revised by RN-677 (ADR 0194): propose_execution_plan gained an execution pipeline — approving the plan records the tasks' module (RN-678) and ACTIVATES execution; the action ends executed or failed, and that outcome is what the Dev Lead reads when resuming. Item 1 below ("has no execute-* pipeline") no longer holds for this type.

    Revised by RN-578 (ADR 0163): EVERY conversational agent's turn stopped holding the GenServer.call — the from is answered on acceptance, and the suspension described here changed only the turn's CLOSING (awaiting_approval instead of agent.done), no longer the moment of the response. A user_message refused during suspension now becomes 409 on the click.

    The first time a conversational agent (Creative, PO, Architect, Dev Lead — all run a synchronous turn via a GenServer.call of up to 180s, mediated by Engine.Agents.TurnoAssincrono) suspends waiting for a human decision. The pattern already existed for the dev agent (RN-073, ADR 0052) and the QA/Infra gates (ADR 0057), but both are triggered by cast and never waited for a synchronous response — that's exactly the point this mechanism solves, and the parallelism ceiling that made the Dev Lead exist (RN-083) is the reason the first suspended approval is its.

    The mechanism, in four pieces:

    1. Engine.Agents.DevLeadTools.run/2 (apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/dev_lead_tools.ex:81-107) calls EngineApiClient.propose_action/5 instead of append_event/3, and returns {:ok, texto} (status executed/auto_approved/approved — all three count as success, because propose_execution_plan has no execute-* pipeline of its own and manual approval stays at "approved" forever), {:pending, action_id}, or {:error, texto}.
    2. Engine.Agents.DevLeadServer.run_turn/2 (apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/dev_lead_server.ex, tool_calls dispatch block) uses Enum.reduce_while to STOP at the first :pending, without processing the following calls nor recursing — and returns the state with the key :aguardando_aprovacao set (%{action_id:, tool_call_id:, tool_name:, remaining:}, the remaining already discounting the suspended iteration against the ceiling). The role: "tool" message does NOT enter state.messages at that moment — writing "pending" there would lie to the model that the command had already answered that (same reasoning as the dev agent, Engine.Harness.Hooks.ActionPipeline).
    3. Engine.Agents.TurnoAssincrono.tratar_resultado/2 (apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/turno_assincrono.ex) answers the from the same way and at the same time as always — that's what breaks the synchronous block at the right moment —, but instead of finalizar/1 (which emits agent.done and agent.status: idle) it calls suspender/1: only agent.status: awaiting_approval, no agent.done, because the turn hasn't ended. The check is by the key's VALUE (Map.get/2, truthy), never by its presence — the Dev Lead carries aguardando_aprovacao: nil since init/1, so the key itself is always present.
    4. Engine.Sessions.LiveBroadcast.agent_status/4 (apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/live_broadcast.ex:38-39) widened the guard from ["working", "idle"] to include "awaiting_approval" — without this, the agent.status from step 3 wouldn't even be persisted.

    The resumption. Engine.Agents.DevLeadServer subscribes Engine.Dev.Wake.subscribe(project_id, "dev-lead") in init/1 — the SAME module that Engine.Gates.QaLeadServer already reuses for the QA subagents, despite the name being "dev": delivery of {:action_settled, ...} is per AGENT, routed by the payload's agentId (apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/dev_agent_wake_worker.ex), not by agent type. When the decision arrives, a handle_info builds the role: "tool" message with the REAL result (texto_do_desfecho/1, same vocabulary as the dev agent and QaLeadServer), clears aguardando_aprovacao, and resumes with TurnoAssincrono.iniciar(state, nil, fn -> run_turn(state, pendente.remaining) end).

    While suspended, a second user_message doesn't start a new turn — a guard on handle_call({:user_message, _text}, _from, %{aguardando_aprovacao: %{}}), tested BEFORE the generic clause, emits agent.error (origin politica) explaining that there's a pending decision in Approvals, without starting any task.

    Accepted gap, declared — restart during the wait. Unlike the dev agent (which rehydrates laco_pendente via handle_continue in init/1, ADR 0052), the Dev Lead does NOT rehydrate aguardando_aprovacao — it's in memory only. If the engine restarts while it's suspended, the decision remains recorded and visible in Approvals (it's durable in the api), but the Dev Lead doesn't narrate the outcome automatically: the process that subscribed to Wake died, and the next restart brings up a new Dev Lead, with no subscription for that action. Closing this would require the same mechanism as ADR 0052 — outside the scope of this change, which only makes the behavior match what packages/contracts/fluxo.yml already declared.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/dev_lead_tools.ex, dev_lead_server.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/turno_assincrono.ex (shared by the four conversational agents); apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/live_broadcast.ex; apps/api/src/domain/actions/decide.ts (propose_execution_plan)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/dev_lead_tools_test.exs, dev_lead_server_test.exs (describe "suspensão em aprovação"), turno_assincrono_test.exs (describe "resultado com :aguardando_aprovacao"), live_broadcast_test.exs, wake_do_outbox_ao_dev_lead_test.exs (the ENTIRE chain — outbox → drain → worker → Engine.Dev.Wake → the Dev Lead's process, same pattern as Engine.Dev.WakeDoOutboxAoAgenteTest), apps/api/test/domain/actions/decide.spec.ts (describe "plano de execução do Dev Lead")
    • ADR: 0086 (revises part of ADR 0053; direct precedent: ADR 0052 and ADR 0057)

    UX Designer — the fifth conversational agent (RN-285..287, ADR 0087)

    This is not a split trigger that fired (packages/contracts/fluxo.yml always declared "when the MANAGED project has its own interface to design" as the criterion) — it is a conscious decision by the product owner to bring the role forward. teste-de- usabilidade (requires a real human user) stays out of reach; metricas-de- uso remains a gap, because it depends on the analytics role, which is still proposto.

    RN-285 — The UX Designer is a SOLO conversational agent, with no area

    Engine.Agents.UxDesignerServer (apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/ux_designer_server.ex:40) mirrors Engine.Agents.ArquitetoServer/DevLeadServer: a GenServer per session, its own bounded tool-use loop with a ceiling of 14 (same caliber as Architect/Dev Lead — a reasoning agent, not light conversation like Creative/PO). Activated by an accepted handoff addressed to "ux-designer" — an already existing GENERIC mechanism (ActivateAgentUseCase/canActivateAgent in the api gained no line at all: any agent with an accepted handoff is already activatable).

    The kickoff (build_kickoff/1, ux_designer_server.ex:222) reads the session's most recent artifact.product_brief — the SAME "business need" the Creative produces, with no new artifact. The design system (design/tokens.css, design/COMPONENTS.md) is DESCRIBED in the identity (Engine.Harness.Agents, entry "ux-designer"), static text, because the conversational agents have no tool for reading files from the repo — it's the only prompt layer present in EVERY turn, not only in the kickoff.

    No area, no subagents: packages/contracts/fluxo.yml already classified the role as camada_produto, alongside Creative and PO, neither of which has an area.

    RN-286 — propose_prototype records an artifact with no table and no dedicated use case, and offers TWO handoffs over the SAME artifact

    artifact.prototipo_navegavel follows the table-less design of artifact.project_image/artifact.c4_diagram (RN-149) — the event log is the record —, but through a DIFFERENT path from the one those two use. choose_project_image/create_c4_diagram need a use case IN THE API (DecidirImagemDoProjetoUseCase/CreateC4DiagramUseCase) because they have content DERIVED from another artifact (the Container level comes from the current module_map) or a domain refusal shared by more than one consumer (the image's resource ceiling). propose_prototype (Engine.Agents.UxDesignerTools.run/2, apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/ux_designer_tools.ex:122) has neither of the two reasons — it's SELF-CONTAINED content that only the UX Designer itself writes and only it reads back —, so FORM validation lives in the engine (Engine.Harness.ArtifactSchemas, type "prototipo_navegavel", artifact_schemas.ex:49/135 — non-empty personas and journeys, at least one screen) and recording uses the GENERIC path the api already exposes for any event type (EngineApiClient.append_event_returning/3), the same mechanism as the Creative's artifact.product_brief. No new route in the api.

    After recording, gravar_e_ofertar_handoffs/2 (ux_designer_tools.ex:149) offers TWO handoffs over the SAME artifact — create_handoff to "po" and to "dev-lead", both with the prototype's artifactId. Never a second artifact for "spec-visual" (packages/contracts/fluxo.yml): the PO reads resumo/prototipo to design the backlog, the Dev Lead reads the SAME telas/anotacoes as a visual implementation reference — duplicating the content would risk the two copies diverging on the next revision, the same argument behind the C4 not retyping the module_map.

    Failing to offer ONE of the two handoffs doesn't undo the already recorded artifact nor the other handoff (RN-116) — the reason comes back as tool-result text, an input to the loop and not the end of the line (RN-163): the model reads it and can report to the user in the next response.

    A SUCCESSFUL propose_prototype ends the turn (ux_designer_server.ex:120, same guard as propose_execution_plan in the Dev Lead) — without it the loop would go back to the model, which could propose again and produce two prototypes with the same total. There's only ONE tool here (there's no second tool call to chain as in the Architect), so "stops at the first success" loses nothing.

    Staff: code ready, dormant for automatic triggering (RN-305/RN-306, ADR 0088)

    This is not a planned phase: packages/contracts/fluxo.yml has declared the Staff/Principal Engineer as status: planned since ADR 0085 ("contract ready, activation decided, awaiting trigger"). The product owner decided to bring the CODE forward even knowing that the automatic trigger (the Anamnese noticing a RECURRING systemic problem) will not fire — the Anamnese is paused (ANAMNESE_ENABLED=false, a product decision of 2026-08-10). See the pending item already documented in RN-086: the same signal that would make the Anamnese propose raising the parallelism ceiling is the one that would make it propose a handoff to the Staff, and neither fires while it is paused.

    RN-305 — The Staff activates through the GENERIC handoff path, without USER_STARTED_AGENTS, and without kickoff/1

    USER_STARTED_AGENTS (apps/api/src/domain/sessions/agent-activation.ts) is the Creative's exception (starts WITHOUT a handoff, by user command) — the Staff does NOT enter it. Investigation confirmed that canActivateAgent already activates any agent with an accepted handoff addressed to it, the same path that already applies to dev-lead/arquiteto/infra; and assertHandoffTargetAllowed (apps/api/src/domain/agents/agent-areas.ts) only refuses a handoff addressed to an area SUBAGENT — the Staff has no area, so no domain change in the api was necessary. "Manually triggerable" means the domain MECHANICS allow it (any agent can call EngineApiClient.create_handoff(..., "staff", ...), and a human accepts through the already existing route), not that there's a dedicated screen today to choose "address handoff to the Staff" — the generic UI for handoff to an agent of choice remains in the backlog (docs/explanation/backlog.md), as it already was before this change.

    Engine.Agents.StaffServer (apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/staff_server.ex) is the fifth solo conversational agent (along with Creative, PO, Architect, Dev Lead), mirroring the Architect — a GenServer per session, rehydration from the event log, bounded tool-use loop with a ceiling of 14 (staff_server.ex:49) — but without kickoff/1: the other leads synthesize an opening instruction from a previous artifact in the session's event log (product_brief, module_map, backlog); the Staff has no such source, because the systemic problem is born outside the session. StaffSupervisor.start_agent/2 brings up the process (rehydrates the history) and it stays idle until the first user_message (staff_server.ex:60) — which is how whoever addressed the handoff explains the problem. apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex:60 (the start/2 clause for "staff") never calls kickoff (the function doesn't even exist), unlike po/arquiteto/dev-lead/infra.

    • Where: apps/api/src/domain/sessions/agent-activation.ts (canActivateAgent, USER_STARTED_AGENTS — untouched), apps/api/src/domain/agents/agent-areas.ts (assertHandoffTargetAllowed — untouched), apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/staff_server.ex, staff_supervisor.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/agent_command_controller.ex (the start/2/message/2/via_for/2 clauses for "staff"), apps/engine/lib/engine/application.ex (Engine.Agents.StaffSupervisor in the supervision tree)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/staff_server_test.exs (rehydration, absence of automatic kickoff — the turn only runs via user_message)
    • ADR: 0088

    RN-306 — propose_rfc records the artifact DIRECTLY and returns the handoff in the SAME tool call, without proposed_action

    Engine.Agents.StaffTools.propose_rfc (apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/staff_tools.ex:41) is the Staff's only tool: problem, options with trade-offs, recommendation, and the scope of a DISPOSABLE PoC (descartavel: true is FIXED — never written by the model). run/2 (staff_tools.ex:89) records artifact.rfc_staff via EngineApiClient.append_event_returning/3 — the same pattern WITHOUT a table and WITHOUT a dedicated use case as Engine.Harness.Tools.EmitInsight (the Architect's emit_insight), and not that of artifact.c4_diagram (ADR 0068), which has its own use case in the api because it DERIVES the Container level from the current module_map — the RFC derives nothing from that side, the whole payload comes from the tool call.

    After recording, the SAME run/2 calls EngineApiClient.create_handoff(..., "staff", "arquiteto", artifact_id) — with no human confirmation in between, same pattern as CriativoServer.executar_confirm_readiness/1 emitting the product_brief and offering the handoff to the PO in the same response. propose_rfc is NOT a proposed_action: recording an architecture document isn't an external effect (it isn't git, terminal, nor agent spend) — the real decision (adopt, adapt, refuse the recommendation) is the Architect's, in the handoff the tool already returns. Failing to create the handoff does NOT take down the process nor lose the already recorded RFC (RN-116) — the reason goes into the tool-result, and the model knows it needs to retry only the handoff, not rewrite the RFC.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/agents/staff_tools.ex
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/agents/staff_server_test.exs ("propõe o RFC, grava o artefato e devolve o handoff ao arquiteto"; "propose_rfc com opções vazias vira tool-result de erro"; "falha ao devolver o handoff ao arquiteto NÃO derruba o processo")
    • ADR: 0088

    Declared, not hidden: the team panel's roster (apps/web/src/lib/agent-status.ts, staffActive) and the dashboard card (ProjectCardSummary.roster, apps/api/.../projects-summary.repository.ts) show the Staff when active, same criterion as infraActive — avoiding the divergence the RosterFacts comment already warned about. SessionPage.tsx was NOT touched: staff stays out of AGENTES_DE_CHAT, the same pattern already accepted for infra (a REAL and ACTIVE lead also outside that list — the handoff to it "is never accepted HERE", as the file's own comment says). Today's end-to-end usage path is the internal route (POST .../agent/message, agent: "staff"), not the Session screen.

    The appsec gets the second secops moment (RN-360/361, ADR 0090)

    packages/contracts/fluxo.yml already declared id: appsec as proposto, with the split criterion written in anticipation: "same pattern as QA: two MOMENTS, not two agents for now". This change builds that second moment — a DESIGN threat model, BEFORE any code/PR exists — a conscious decision by the product owner to bring the activation forward, without waiting for the implementavel gate that the registry itself cited as the trigger.

    RN-360 — The DESIGN threat model runs over story + module_map, without worktree/task_id, in the SAME secops process

    Whoever runs appsec is the SAME Engine.Gates.SecOpsAgentServer that already exists for the deterministic PR verdict — not a new process. run_design/2 (apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/secops_agent_server.ex:76) is a GenServer.cast to the same Registry key ({project_id, "secops"}) that run/2 already uses; the handle_cast({:run_design, story_id}, state) (secops_agent_server.ex:93) fetches the context via Engine.Gates.AppSecContextBuilder.fetch/2 (apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/appsec_context_builder.ex:31) — the story in the project's backlog (EngineApiClient.list_backlog/1, the SAME read that RN-164 gave the PO) and the current module_map of the session that created the story (EngineApiClient.get_infra_context/2, the SAME read without task/story that the Infra area already does) — and then calls Engine.Gates.AppSecAgent.run/3 (apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/appsec_agent.ex:47).

    AppSecAgent is a STATELESS module (not a GenServer, same shape as QaPerformanceSegurancaAgent), with a tool registry WITHOUT Terminal — [ReadFile, SearchWorkspace, EmitThreatModel] — running a STRIDE-lite checklist (Spoofing/Tampering/Repudiation/Information disclosure/Denial of service/Elevation of privilege) via Engine.Harness.ToolLoop.run/1. The structural difference of the "second moment without Terminal, without task_id" from the earlier gate pattern: no dev_state/worktree_path enters the ctx — ReadFile/SearchWorkspace degrade to the fallback of Engine.Actions.Workspace.workspace_dir/1 (the project's SHARED checkout, not an agent worktree), and session_id comes from story["sessionId"], never from a dev_state. EmitThreatModel (apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/tools/emit_threat_model.ex) has no approved/changes_requested verdict — appsec always ENDS by recording the threat model, never approving/failing anything —, and that's why it doesn't reuse Engine.Gates.Hooks.Termination (the extracted shape is different): it has its OWN hook, Engine.Gates.Hooks.AppSecTermination.

    Finished successfully, run_appsec_design/3 (secops_agent_server.ex:234) emits artifact.threat_model (storyId/threatModel/requisitosDeSeguranca/riscos, schema in apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/artifact_schemas.ex:71 — riscos stays out of the required keys because an empty list is a valid answer). Failure (iteration ceiling, budget, or the model stopping without calling emit_threat_model) becomes a durable agent.error with origin (modelo/politica/infra), same ruler as RN-059 — never an empty response nor silence only in broadcast.

    The automatic trigger exists since RN-539. This delivery was kept self-contained on purpose (no file of another front touched: decide.ts, apps/api/config/gates.yml, and dev_lead_tools.ex untouched), and declared the gap: run_design/2 was TRIGGERABLE and nothing triggered it on its own. The trigger point it named — the Dev Lead's assess_implementability, front qa-estrategia, gate implementavel, same ADR 0090 — became the real caller, in parallel and only once per story.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/secops_agent_server.ex (run_design/2, handle_cast/2, run_appsec_design/3, emit_threat_model/3, emit_bloqueio_appsec/3); apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/appsec_agent.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/appsec_context_builder.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/tools/emit_threat_model.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/hooks/appsec_termination.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/artifact_schemas.ex (schema threat_model)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/gates/appsec_agent_test.exs, apps/engine/test/engine/gates/appsec_context_builder_test.exs, apps/engine/test/engine/gates/secops_agent_server_test.exs (the three run_design tests)
    • Origin: ADR 0090

    RN-361 — The completed threat model creates THREE handoffs, always addressing the LEAD

    criar_handoffs_appsec/3 (secops_agent_server.ex:326) creates one handoff per target declared in packages/contracts/fluxo.yml (saidas of appsec): arquiteto, dev-lead, and infra — same pattern as OfferInfraHandoffUseCase/ArquitetoServer.executar_offer_infra_handoff/1 (SEPARATE calls, one per target, so that one handoff failure doesn't undo the other two already created). The flow id is area-infra, but the addressable AGENT is "infra" (apps/api/src/domain/agents/agent-areas.ts — lead: 'infra'), never area-infra: an external handoff addresses only the area's LEAD (ADR 0038), and CreateHandoffUseCase.assertHandoffTargetAllowed would refuse a toAgent that doesn't resolve to a lead/agent-with-no-area. Failure of ONE target becomes an agent.error narrated per target (RN-116) — the other two handoffs already created are not undone.

    Since RN-636 the three are ASKED, no longer always created: a destination that already has a pending offer or is already active in the project doesn't get another one, and that isn't a failure.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/gates/secops_agent_server.ex:55 (@appsec_handoff_targets), :377 (criar_handoffs_appsec/3)
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine/gates/secops_agent_server_test.exs ("run_design: threat model concluído emite artifact.threat_model e cria os TRÊS handoffs")
    • Origin: ADR 0090

    "N agents online" on the dashboard — LIVE status, never historical presence (RN-409, ADR 0097)

    RN-414 — rag_search: agents get the tool the product had never exposed

    grep -rn "rag" apps/engine/lib gave ZERO occurrences before this delivery — the complete RAG (pgvector, hybrid search) was only consumed by the "Chat RAG" web tab. The new tool rag_search (apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/rag_search.ex, category :direct, reading isn't an external effect) calls POST /internal/rag/search — a new route that REUSES HybridSearchUseCase without duplicating the search logic — and returns hits formatted with citation (path + excerpt), always with degraded: true visible at the START of the text when embedding wasn't available (never hidden by the ceiling cut).

    Its own ceilings, in the spirit of RN-150: top_k clamped to 10 within the tool itself (it doesn't trust what the api would accept), a 16 KiB ceiling on the formatted text (smaller than the 32 KiB of search_workspace/read_file — each RAG hit is already a whole chunk+excerpt). A network failure becomes an error readable by the model, never a ToolLoop crash (RN-163).

    Registered in Engine.Harness.Tools (PO/Architect/conversational) and Engine.Dev.Tools (dev agent); also extended to the read gates that already cite indexed ADRs/conventions (QA-automation, QA-strategy, AppSec, QA-performance/security) — not to Infra.WorkflowsAgent nor Psychologist/Anamnese, which reason over the event log, not over the project's docs/code.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/tools/rag_search.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/sessions/engine_api_client.ex (rag_search/4); apps/api/src/interfaces/http/internal/internal-rag.controller.ts
    • Test: rag_search_test.exs (hits formatted with citation; visible degradation; network failure without crash; byte ceiling truncating with a clear mark; top_k clamp); engine_api_client_rag_test.exs
    • Origin: see ADR 0100

    RN-417 — Psychologist and Anamnese query by relevance, with declared degradation to recency

    Psychologist.ContextBuilder/Anamnese.ContextBuilder keep reading what they always read (recent events / time window), and gain a SECOND source, EngineApiClient.rag_search/4, with a query derived from the analysis's TRIGGER — the already classified termination cause, in the Psychologist; catalog competencies still without current_profile, in the Anamnese (NEVER free text of a hypothesis/rationale, by the already established prohibition on the Anamnese ever inferring health/personality/age/gender — the query only contains competency/member/project names). The hits enter the EXISTING Triage budget (max_prompt_events/max_payload_chars) — they take slots from the recents window, never adding outside it.

    The RAG call is strictly ADDITIVE: with no hit (RAG unavailable, error, or simply no result), the behavior is IDENTICAL to before this RN — no pre-existing test needed to change. degraded: true (RAG fell back to lexical-only for lack of embedding) appears EXPLICITLY in the final context, in both agents — never hidden by the ceiling cut.

    The kickoffs of the Psychologist (psychologist-kickoff) and the Anamnese (anamnese-kickoff) now resolve as a graph TEMPLATE (EngineApiClient.get_prompt_template/2) when graph_templates_enabled? is on (GRAPH_TEMPLATES_ENABLED, default false) — with a mandatory fallback to the inline text on any failure (api down, template not seeded, flag off). :pinned => true stays the same on both paths.

    The THIRD consumer of the same flag is the ContextManager's summarization prompt (context-manager-summarize, the fourth and last template of the RN-413 batch — it was the only one still without a consumer, a gap that ADR 0101 declared open and that closes here). Same ruler, byte for byte: flag on tries the template and replaces {{turnos}} with the SAME <role>: <content> body the inline path already built; any outcome that isn't {"body" => corpo} with a non-empty binary body falls to the inline — WITHOUT an error and WITHOUT an error log, because flag off, template not yet seeded, and the graph api down are the SAME degradation path. With the flag off (the default) the api isn't even called. The render is its OWN private function, not a shared helper: the set of placeholders belongs to the template, not to the mechanism — each of the three consumers has its own. And the deterministic fallback for when the MODEL fails ("(N turnos anteriores omitidos)") is something else and does NOT go through any template: it's code behavior, not prompt text, and the template's own .md declares so.

    Since RN-621 the summary prompt, on both paths, asks that each turn keep its original language and that quotation and code not be translated; the versioned template went up to version "2".

    Consumption of the rest of the graph (query_user_context — hypotheses with evidence and profiles read DIRECTLY from Neo4j) stays DECLARED out of this delivery: still with no exposed HTTP route on the api side; Psychologist/Anamnese today only query RAG (pgvector) via rag_search, not the relations graph itself.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/psychologist/context_builder.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/psychologist_worker.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/anamnese/context_builder.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/workers/anamnese_worker.ex; apps/engine/lib/engine/harness/context_manager.ex (prompt/1, render_template/2, prompt_inline/1)
    • Test: context_builder_test.exs of both agents (hits present; RAG failure degrades without error; degraded: true visible; top_k clamp; query derived from the trigger); psychologist_worker_test.exs/ anamnese_worker_test.exs (template with success and with fallback; :pinned identical on both paths; flag off never calls the api); context_manager_test.exs (template with {{turnos}} replaced; {:error, :not_found} falls to the inline and compaction ends normally; flag off never calls get_prompt_template)
    • Origin: see ADR 0101

    RN-419 — The local runner authenticates with a single-use ticket, scoped to project and role

    REVISED by RN-421 (ADR 0104): the ticket's refusal condition cited below (workspaceMode !== 'local') became executionMode !== 'runner' — same ruler, new name. The rest of this entry still holds as is.

    The terminal:<projectId> channel (new Phoenix socket, /runner) receives two distinct roles — :runner (the CLI on the user's machine, at most ONE per project, exclusivity guaranteed by :global.register_name/3) and :web (the Terminal tab, multiple simultaneous) — and both come in authenticated by a SINGLE-USE TICKET, the same security pattern as RN-108 (session socket ticket), with an ownership inversion: the ticket is ISSUED BY THE ENGINE (runner_socket_tickets, schema "engine", its own Ecto migration — not an extension of the session ticket table, which belongs to the api), and the API requests it via a new internal HTTP route (POST /internal/projects/:projectId/runner-tickets) — the inverse of the session ticket flow, where the api writes directly into its own table.

    POST /projects/:projectId/runner-ticket (role developer+, refuses if workspaceMode !== 'local') and POST /projects/:projectId/terminal-ticket (role viewer+, any mode) are the two public routes that issue the ticket to the right client — the runner asks for the first, the web asks for the second. The ticket is consumed (validation + single-use conditional UPDATE) at the channel's join; a ticket from ANOTHER project or reused never opens the topic.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/runners/socket_ticket.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/runners/registry.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine_web/channels/{runner_socket,terminal_channel}.ex; apps/api/src/interfaces/http/runner/runner-tickets.controller.ts, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/runner/request-runner-ticket.use-case.ts
    • Test: runner_socket_test.exs/terminal_channel_test.exs (join with a valid ticket succeeds; reused ticket refuses; a ticket from another project doesn't open the wrong topic; a second :runner on the same project is refused; a second runner manages to connect after the first drops); request-runner-ticket.use-case.spec.ts/ runner-tickets.controller.spec.ts (refuses for a non-local project on the runner ticket; the terminal ticket works for any mode)
    • Origin: see ADR 0103

    RN-420 — An agent command routed to the runner goes through the SAME approval pipeline; PTY is a user action, audited

    REVISED by RN-423 (ADR 0104): the condition to route is no longer binary. workspace_mode == "local" became execution_mode == "runner" AND non-null workspace_verified_at AND runner connected — missing any of the THREE, the command is explicitly REFUSED, never falling into the System.cmd/bind-mount of mounted (which doesn't exist for a runner project, with no bind-mount at all). The rest of this entry — the usual approval pipeline, PTY as a user action — still holds as is.

    Engine.Actions.TerminalExecutor decides to route a command to the runner (instead of the usual System.cmd, inside the container) AFTER the normal pipeline (decide()/proposed_action) has already approved — routing is only a choice of DESTINATION for the same already authorized command, never a second execution path escaping the policy (which keeps the absolute ceilings of RN-418 applying the same, sudo/git included). Condition to route (history, see revision above): workspace_mode == "local" AND runner connected (Engine.Runners.Registry.connected?/1) — without either of the two, the usual behavior continues (System.cmd in the container via bind-mount, ADR 0072 becomes the FALLBACK, never removed).

    Interactive PTY is DIFFERENT: it's an action of the authenticated USER typing in the terminal of their own machine, not the agent's — that's why it does NOT go through proposed_action. But it needs to leave a trace: pty_open/pty_close coming from the web emit terminal.session.started/terminal.session.ended in the event log (addressed to the project's most recent session, ProjectSession.latest_id/1, the same mechanism the Anamnese already uses to narrate something project-scoped). A real finding during consolidation: with no runner connected, the web needs an EXPLICIT pty_error back — discarding the request with only a log on the server would leave the tab stuck in "loading" forever, never reaching the "no runner" state the screen already knew how to show.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine/actions/terminal_executor.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine/runners/runner_router.ex, apps/engine/lib/engine_web/channels/terminal_channel.ex
    • Test: terminal_executor_test.exs (routes to the runner with local mode + runner connected; cwd is passed along; falls to the current path without a connected runner; never routes in container mode); terminal_channel_test.exs (pty_open without a connected runner returns pty_error to the web, never stays without an answer)
    • Origin: see ADR 0103

    The Runner folder explorer becomes three columns, and creating a runner project no longer waits for the final step (RN-436/436)

    RN-454 — The Insights tab knows the Psychologist is paused BEFORE the user runs into the 503

    Found by USE: the Insights tab, with zero hypotheses, showed "No hypotheses yet — the Psychologist analyzes each closed session" even with PSYCHOLOGIST_ENABLED=false — the same sentence that appears when the Psychologist is ACTIVE and just hasn't run yet. The two situations are indistinguishable by the text, which is the same class of defect RN-088/RN-107 already closed for other screens: a state that exists and that the product knows, but doesn't show.

    RN-117 already covered discovering the pause, but only on the CLICK of "Reanalyze" (503 → PsychologistDisabledError) — and that button only exists in the analyses strip, which only appears when runs.length > 0. A session with no hypothesis at all never gets near it, so the pause was invisible precisely on the empty screen.

    GET /internal/psychologist/status (engine, PsychologistCommandController.status/2) is a pure read of PsychologistWorker.enabled?/0 — WITH NO side effect, unlike /reanalyze, which creates a job when enabled. The api exposes GET /projects/:projectId/psychologist/status (GetPsychologistStatusUseCase, role:viewer) on top of that — the project in the URL only for consistency with the sibling routes (hypotheses, psychologist/analyses); the flag itself is GLOBAL, as RN-117 already records. ProjectInsightsTab.tsx consumes that read (usePsychologistStatus) and chooses the empty state's sentence by it: paused shows "The Psychologist is paused — no session is analyzed until it's reactivated" (insights.projectInsightsTab.emptyPaused); otherwise, it keeps the original sentence, which IS honest when the feature is indeed active. The persistent warning (pausedNotice) and the "Reanalyze" buttons also now reflect that proactive read, and not only the useState discovered by the 503 — which still exists, as reinforcement, for the rare case of the flag changing IN FLIGHT between the status read and the click.

    • Where: apps/engine/lib/engine_web/controllers/psychologist_command_controller.ex (status/2), apps/engine/lib/engine_web/router.ex (GET /internal/psychologist/status), apps/api/src/application/ports/api-to-engine-client.port.ts (getPsychologistStatus), apps/api/src/infrastructure/http-clients/api-to-engine-client.ts, apps/api/src/application/use-cases/execution/get-psychologist-status.use-case.ts, apps/api/src/interfaces/http/psychologist/psychologist.controller.ts (status), apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts (getPsychologistStatus), apps/web/src/lib/hooks.ts (usePsychologistStatus), apps/web/src/routes/ProjectInsightsTab.tsx
    • Test: apps/engine/test/engine_web/controllers/psychologist_command_controller_test.exs (status/2, enabled: true/false), apps/api/test/infrastructure/http-clients/api-to-engine-client.spec.ts (getPsychologistStatus), apps/api/test/application/use-cases/execution/get-psychologist-status.use-case.spec.ts, apps/web/src/routes/ProjectInsightsTab.test.tsx
    • Edge case: the flag remains GLOBAL (see RN-117) — the api route accepts projectId in the URL only to match the pattern of the controller's sibling routes, and not because the response varies per project.
    • Origin: found by real USE navigating the Insights tab with PSYCHOLOGIST_ENABLED=false, not a script. No ADR — a one-off extension of the already existing read mechanism of RN-117, the same pattern as RN-088/ RN-107 for the rest of the product.
